

## Allerton, Bronx — /fire-alarm-installation-allerton-bronx-ny (factory research, 2026-10-27)

### A. Fire alarm installation in Allerton: local angles (most important)

**Building types and what installers meet there**
- 2910 Wallace Avenue is a 98-unit, 6-story Class A residential building in Allerton, built in 1931. It had a 5-alarm fire on January 10, 2025, which partially collapsed about 90% of the roof. — https://augrented.com/nyc/2045490006-2910-wallace-avenue-bronx
- The Wallace Avenue fire is the main local case study. The roof and all top-floor apartments were destroyed, and water damaged every other unit. More than 250 tenants were displaced. — https://www.cbsnews.com/newyork/news/fire-in-the-bronx-allerton/ and https://www.norwoodnews.org/update-allerton-bronx-tenants-from-2910-wallace-avenue-demand-city-action-to-return-home-after-devastating-fire/
- A resident of the Wallace Avenue building said, "I would have expected a lot more smoke inside the building for the way it looked outside of the building." Neither this nor the other sources say whether the alarm system worked, so don't claim that. — https://abc7ny.com/amp/post/2-people-hurt-5-alarm-bronx-apartment-fire-high-winds-fuel-flames-raise-difficulting-firefighters/15786101/
- Another top-floor fire hit a six-story building on Bronx Park East near Waring Avenue. A man was found unconscious in a top-floor apartment. A passerby rang the intercoms for the fifth and sixth floors and kicked in the front door to alert people. All the D-line apartments got vacate orders. This is a local example of intercom and front-door access being used to warn residents. — https://bronx.news12.com/person-critically-injured-in-fire-at-6-story-building-in-allerton and https://bronx.news12.com/allerton-apartment-fire-leaves-1-seriously-injured-12-residents-displaced
- Mixed-use corner buildings with a shop below and apartments above are a separate case. On Jan 28, 2026, a fire at 700 Adee Avenue off White Plains Road reached four alarms. FDNY described the building as a 3-story non-fireproofed structure with a deli on the first floor and two apartments above. It was connected to a tire shop. Two families upstairs evacuated after smelling smoke. — https://www.norwoodnews.org/update-allerton-fdny-tackling-all-hands-early-morning-adee-ave-fire-no-injuries-to-date/ and https://abc7ny.com/post/flames-shoot-deli-apartments-allerton-section-bronx/18491380/
- The Adee Avenue building had a bodega and an auto shop on the ground floor. Neighbors called it a very popular corner store. — https://www.cbsnews.com/newyork/news/bronx-fire-home-deli-allerton-throggs-neck/
- Two-family and small multi-family houses also burn. On March 14, 2026, 79 fire and EMS workers responded to 2536 Matthews Ave for a second-floor apartment fire. — https://abc7ny.com/post/1-hurt-amid-house-fire-allerton-section-bronx/18714357/
- Allerton also has NYCHA complexes. Boston Road Plaza is one 20-story building. Eastchester Gardens has ten 7- and 8-story buildings. Parkside Houses has 14 buildings of 6 to 15 stories. Pelham Parkway Houses has twenty-three 6-story buildings. Any work in these would go through NYCHA, which I did not research. — https://kids.kiddle.co/Allerton,_Bronx
- Pre-war Art Deco and Tudor co-ops sit along Pelham Parkway North and the side streets. Examples are 601 Pelham Parkway North, a 7-story, 116-unit pre-war co-op built in 1937 and 655 Pelham Parkway North, an 86-unit, 6-story pre-war building from 1931. Architects and builders in the 1920s and 30s competed on distinctive entries. Decorative lobbies and entryways may limit where devices and conduit can go. This is my inference, not something a source states. — https://www.corcoran.com/building/allerton/117841, https://www.bhsusa.com/for-rent/bronx/new-york-city/other-bronx/655-pelham-parkway-north-4f-new-york-city-ny-10467/RLS10533937-1569084 and https://www.untappedcities.com/10-beautiful-apartment-entryways-of-the-bronx/
- Postwar mid-rise stock: Waring Hall Condos is a 6-story, 122-unit building completed in 1960. — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/allerton-neighborhood/

**City data (HPD, 311, DOB)**
- 2910 Wallace Avenue had a long record before the fire. City records show 239 heat-related complaints over the last three years. As of July 2025, city records showed 73 total complaints, two of them open. — https://www.fox5ny.com/news/nyc-bronx-fire-today-news-now-apartment-building and https://www.norwoodnews.org/update-allerton-bronx-tenants-from-2910-wallace-avenue-demand-city-action-to-return-home-after-devastating-fire/
- The owner lost a court bid to leave HPD's heat-monitoring program for landlords with chronic heat complaints. Displaced residents said some tenants used space heaters and stoves for warmth, a potential fire risk. The cause was still under investigation in those reports. — https://www.thecityreporter.nyc/2025/01/14/ved-parkash-bronx-fire-hpd-heat-sensors-program/ and https://www.thecityreporter.nyc/newsletter/landlord-of-burned-building-sued-to-stop-heat-monitoring/
- Records show electrical issues, and recurring elevator violations including door-lock monitoring. The same address page lists active DOB violations for failure to maintain the building after the fire. — https://augrented.com/nyc/2045490006-2910-wallace-avenue-bronx
- Council Member Riley questioned HPD about the violations at 2910 Wallace Avenue at an April 2025 Fire and Emergency Management hearing. He said there were many violations residents had complained about for years. — https://citymeetings.nyc/meetings/new-york-city-council/2025-04-03-1000-am-committee-on-fire-and-emergency-management/chapter/council-member-riley-questions-hpd-about-2910-wallace-avenue-violations/
- Allerton-wide HPD, 311 and DOB permit counts for fire alarms or smoke detectors: EMPTY. I found no neighborhood-level figures.

**Rules that change how the work is done**
- Design and installation documents for fire alarm systems must be submitted to FDNY for review and approval before installation. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/fdny/downloads/pdf/about/Chapter-09.pdf
- Replacing detectors, pull stations or control panels with identical parts and no design change counts as a repair. Moving or replacing a fire alarm control panel or similar control units needs new plans filed with FDNY. — https://asmintegrators.com/fire-alarm-code/
- One-way voice communication must be installed in buildings over 125 feet. The height is measured from the lowest level of fire department vehicle access to the highest occupied floor. Most Allerton walk-ups are 6 stories and nowhere near this. The 20-story Boston Road Plaza is the one local building that could be affected. — https://sparcfp.com/nyc-dob-buildings-bulletin-for-fire-alarm-work-in-existing-buildings/
- The 2022 NYC Building Code does not require a manual fire alarm system in buildings of two stories or fewer, if each dwelling unit is separated by 1-hour fire partitions and has direct exit to a public way, exit court or yard. This is relevant to Allerton's two-family houses. — https://up.codes/viewer/new_york_city/nyc-building-code-2022/chapter/9/fire-protection-systems
- NYC landmarks (LPC) rules: EMPTY. I found no source on a landmark district or landmark building in Allerton.
- Reddit threads about fire alarms or buildings in Allerton: EMPTY. My searches turned up no relevant threads.
- Entry-system and AV details for specific Allerton buildings: EMPTY, except the intercom and front-door detail in the Bronx Park East fire above.

**Where it matters: blocks and businesses**
- The busiest commercial stretch is Allerton Avenue, the seven blocks between Barker Avenue and Matthews Avenue. It has florists, salons, banks, gyms, locally owned restaurants and dozens of delis. Grocers include Key Food, Foodtown and Food Choice Market. — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/allerton-neighborhood/
- Other commercial pockets: the shopping district at Allerton Avenue and White Plains Road and the corner at Adee Avenue and White Plains Road, where the January 2026 fire happened. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allerton_Avenue_station
- The White Plains Road BID, established in 1994, covers a four-block area with over 100 stores, from Bolton Street east along Pelham Parkway South to White Plains Road and south to Brady Avenue. Part of it overlaps the Pelham Parkway side of Allerton. — https://pelhamparkway.com/about-us/
- Blocks tied to fire incidents: Wallace Avenue near Pelham Parkway, Bronx Park East near Waring Avenue, Matthews Avenue, and Adee Avenue at White Plains Road (sources above).

### B. Neighborhood background

**History and development**
- Before 1900 the land was rural Town of Westchester, annexed to New York City in 1895. The New York, Westchester & Boston Railway came early in the 20th century. By the 1910s, speculators advertised "country homes in the Bronx" along Allerton Avenue. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/allerton
- The neighborhood is named for farmer Daniel Allerton (1818–1877). The city built Allerton Avenue across former estate land in 1914. — https://cornerfacts.com/new-york/bronx/allerton/
- A law-firm history page says the Lorillard family owned the land and sold parts of the estate in the early 1900s. I'd treat this as weaker than the other sources. — https://banvillelaw.com/the-history-of-the-bronx-neighborhood-allerton/
- The Allerton Avenue subway station opened March 3, 1917, built under the Dual Contracts. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allerton_Avenue_station
- After WWII, the area east of Boston Post Road was mostly Italian immigrants, and the area west of it was an older Jewish community. West Indian residents arrived in the 1980s, and Dominicans settled west of Bronxwood Avenue around the same time. — https://kids.kiddle.co/Allerton,_Bronx
- Today the area south of Allerton Avenue and east of Bronxwood Avenue has many Italian-Americans and Albanian-Americans, as well as Asian, Hispanic and West Indian residents. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allerton,_Bronx
- Boundaries are disputed. City Planning has labeled the southern part "Laconia" and the northern part "Bronxdale." — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allerton,_Bronx
- Sources disagree on which part is called Laconia or Bronxwood. One source gives Bronxwood to the south and Laconia to the north. — https://nycneighborhoods.app/map/neighborhoods/allerton-bronx/

**Main streets**
- Allerton Avenue is the main east–west artery. White Plains Road carries the elevated 2 and 5 trains. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/allerton
- Allerton Avenue runs from Bronx Park East to Williamsbridge Road. Waring Avenue and Pelham Parkway are the cross-town routes to the south (same source).

**Building stock**
- Half of the 1,463 residential lots hold buildings from 1945 or earlier, and 46% of those date from before 1940. The 2020 Census counted 34,623 residents. — https://cornerfacts.com/new-york/bronx/allerton/
- Housing is mostly brick-clad mid-rise apartment buildings, with over 80 percent of residents renting. Multifamily houses are more common east of U.S. 1. A few detached single-family colonial revivals exist. — https://www.homes.com/bronx-ny/allerton-neighborhood/
- South of Pelham Parkway there are brick two-family homes and attached rowhouses. That page is about the neighboring Pelham Parkway neighborhood. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/pelham-parkway
- Landmark districts: EMPTY.

**Parks and landmarks**
- Bronx Park lies to the west, home to the Bronx Zoo and the New York Botanical Garden. Local green space includes Waring Playground, Reiss Field, and the 109-acre Pelham Parkway. — https://www.homes.com/bronx-ny/allerton-neighborhood/
- The NYPL Allerton branch has served the area from Barnes Avenue since 1959. — https://cornerfacts.com/new-york/bronx/allerton/

**What it is known for**
- It is a working-class, mostly renter neighborhood with Italian and Albanian roots and West Indian and Dominican communities (sources above). The Allerton Avenue shopping strip is its commercial hub (Homes.com above).

**Recent local news on security and building safety**
- Pelham Parkway Houses: in 2014 there were plans to add 375 security cameras, two years after city funding was approved following a murder in the complex. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelham_Parkway_Houses
- Wallace Avenue aftermath: dozens of tenants rallied for help two months after the fire. — https://bronx.news12.com/2025/03/11/tenants-rally-for-help-2-months-after-a-5-alarm-fire-ripped-through-their-allerton-apartment-building/4RlPFVM81Fg0Fp7OAyP2xV
- Beyond Allerton, other Bronx fires in 2026 were in Fordham (May) and Morris Heights (September). In the Fordham fire, a resident said they heard their fire alarm going off. — https://www.cbsnews.com/newyork/news/bronx-fire-fordham-child-killed/ and https://www.cbsnews.com/newyork/news/fire-bronx-apartment-building-fdny/

**Gaps (EMPTY)**
- Reddit discussion
- LPC or landmark rules
- Allerton-specific DOB permit data
- Allerton-specific 311 data on smoke alarms
- Smart-home or entry-system news

## Bergen Beach, Brooklyn — /fire-alarm-installation-bergen-beach-brooklyn-ny (factory research, 2026-10-27)

I couldn't find any HPD, 311, DOB, LPC, or Reddit material specific to Bergen Beach, so those bullets say EMPTY. Where a bullet is my own inference rather than something a source states, I say so.

### A. Fire alarm installation: Bergen Beach-specific angles

**Building types and entry/wiring situations**
- Bergen Beach and Georgetowne are both zoned as predominantly residential, with one- and two-family homes. Most jobs are therefore house-scale smoke/CO and interconnected detection, not high-rise systems. — https://www.nyc.gov/site/brooklyncb18/about/bergen-beach-georgetowne.page
- The interior streets (Avenue T, Bergen Avenue, Royce Street) are mostly low-rise brick and stucco houses built after WWII, with driveways and lawns. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/bergen-beach
- Listings include completely detached stucco two-family homes with a rental studio and a 4-bedroom duplex over a finished basement. Another is a corner two-family on Avenue N: owner's 4-bed over a 1-bed tenant apartment, over a finished basement with a separate entrance. My inference: these owner/tenant and basement-apartment layouts call for interconnected detection across units and the basement. — https://www.homes.com/brooklyn-ny/bergen-beach-neighborhood/multi-family-homes-for-sale/ and https://www.homes.com/brooklyn-ny/bergen-beach-neighborhood/
- Semi-detached homes are common. One example is a semi-detached brick two-family on a 26' x 100' lot. — https://www.homes.com/brooklyn-ny/bergen-beach-neighborhood/multi-family-homes-for-sale/
- Three-family and larger homes exist. One listing is a three-family at 1166 Bergen Avenue. — https://www.redfin.com/neighborhood/224213/NY/New-York/Bergen-Beach
- Condos are a smaller stock. Mill Harbor Condominiums is one of the newest projects in Bergen Beach. The Georgetown section has numerous gated communities, including Mill Harbor. Gates may affect FDNY and technician access (my inference). — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/brooklyn-ny/bergen-beach-neighborhood/ and https://brooklyneagle.com/303032/bergen-beach-history/
- Condo listings sit at addresses such as 1072 E 73rd St, 1091 and 1093 E 72nd St, and 1070 E 73rd St. — https://www.redfin.com/neighborhood/224213/NY/New-York/Bergen-Beach/condos
- Basements are a recurring feature of the housing. Redfin has a "basements" listing category with dozens of Bergen Beach addresses. — https://www.redfin.com/neighborhood/224213/NY/New-York/Bergen-Beach/basements
- Building age varies. hlres lists 6710 Avenue T, built in 1899, as the oldest building in the neighborhood. Sold listings include a 1955 detached cape. Another is a detached 1930 house on a 40x100 lot. Older wiring in pre-war homes is a plausible concern (my inference). — https://www.hlres.com/buildings/neighborhood/66/bergen-beach and https://www.homes.com/brooklyn-ny/bergen-beach-neighborhood/sold/

**Local fire and flood context**
- A recent local incident: on 1/30/26 at 2276 East 73rd St (corner of Avenue W) there was a phone-alarm report of a fire in the boiler room in the basement of a private dwelling. The FDNY forum post lists it as an all-hands Box 4178. It supports a basement/boiler-room detection angle. — https://nycfire.net/forums/threads/1-30-26-brooklyn-all-hands-box-4178.92337/
- Flood risk matters for equipment placement. Most residences are at extreme risk from flooding because the neighborhood is surrounded by three basins. Over 200 properties were affected during Hurricane Sandy. Basement panels and low-mounted devices are the likely concern (my inference). — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/brooklyn-ny/bergen-beach-neighborhood/
- Fire at the original resort: a fire nearly destroyed the Bergen Beach amusement resort on April 17, 1904. The only water source was the bay, and the tide was out. This works as a short local-history hook. — http://lostamusementparks.napha.org/Articles/NewYork/BergenBeach.html
- Belt Parkway car fire: on April 21, a phone alarm box reported a car fire on the eastbound Belt Parkway near the Jamaica Bay Riding Academy. The listing doesn't state the year. This is vehicle-related, not a building fire. — https://citizen.com/-OqmqRwOeEOwfVZzPWbV
- Citywide lithium-ion battery fire trend (not Bergen Beach-specific): FDNY said battery-caused fires were up about 70% this year. The cited Aug. 31, 2026 fatal fire was at NYCHA's Albany Houses in Crown Heights, so it is not local. — https://www.firehouse.com/community-risk/investigation-equipment/video/55401712/lithium-ion-battery-eyed-in-deadly-brooklyn-blaze-fdny

**Commercial angle**
- Georgetowne Shopping Center at 2181 Ralph Ave is a 147,000 sq ft center on nearly 9 acres, with 30 retailers and 575 parking spaces. Key tenants include IHOP, Starbucks, Five Guys, GNC, Chipotle, and Five Below. Food-service tenants are the likely driver of commercial-kitchen and tenant-fit-out alarm work (my inference). — https://api-federalrealty.reol.com/pdf?file_name=Georgetowne+Shopping+Center+-+Brochure.pdf&id=032ebdc598c387496e228b2db6716e329dd64583
- Food Way Georgetowne is at 2149 Ralph Avenue. — https://www.foodwayshop.com/
- Mill Basin Bagel Cafe is at 6319 Avenue N. — https://foursquare.com/top-places/brooklyn/best-places-baked-fish
- The neighborhood is almost entirely residential with a couple of stores. Commercial alarm work is therefore concentrated in a few spots. — https://italianenclaves.org/neighborhoods/east-coast/new-york/brooklyn/mill-basin-bergen-beach/
- Assembly occupancies: the Church of St. Bernard on East 68th near Avenue U was established as a parish in 1961. Saint Bernard is technically in Bergen Beach. — https://forgotten-ny.com/2008/11/bergen-beach-georgetown-brooklyn/ and https://italianenclaves.org/neighborhoods/east-coast/new-york/brooklyn/mill-basin-bergen-beach/

**City data and other discussion**
- HPD violations specific to Bergen Beach — EMPTY
- 311 complaints (smoke/CO, alarm) specific to Bergen Beach — EMPTY
- DOB permits and fire alarm filings specific to Bergen Beach — EMPTY
- LPC rules or landmark districts in Bergen Beach — EMPTY. No landmark district turned up in any source.
- Reddit threads about fire alarms in Bergen Beach — EMPTY
- Local news on Bergen Beach alarm or fire code enforcement — EMPTY
- Nextdoor has a Bergen Beach neighborhood page. One area (Ave M-72St) shows 71% homeowners. I found no fire-alarm threads. — https://nextdoor.com/neighborhood/bergenbeach1--brooklyn--ny/
- Competitor page note: one competitor's Bergen Beach page claims headquarters at 1282 Troy Ave and lists brownstones, row houses, walk-ups and NYCHA complexes. The same template says it is headquartered at that address in Brighton Beach. Its Bergen Beach copy is generic and mismatched, so a page grounded in detached and semi-detached homes, basements and flood risk would stand apart. — https://abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-bergen-beach-brooklyn-ny.html and https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-brighton-beach-brooklyn-ny

### B. History and development
- Early history: Norwegian shipwright Hans Hansen Bergen bought farmland there in the 1600s, when it was in the town of Flatlands. The area was then known as Bergen Island. — https://brooklyneagle.com/303028/how-bergen-beach-got-its-name/
- Resort era: From 1896 to 1919, Percy G. Williams and Thomas Adams ran an amusement park there. The NAPHA article gives the dates as 1896–1918. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bergen_Beach,_Brooklyn and http://lostamusementparks.napha.org/Articles/NewYork/BergenBeach.html
- Williams was a theater builder, and Adams was of Chiclets chewing gum fame. — https://brooklyneagle.com/303028/how-bergen-beach-got-its-name/
- Landfill: It was connected to the rest of Brooklyn via landfill by 1918, though development did not come for many years. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bergen_Beach,_Brooklyn
- Residential plans: A residential community was planned in 1925 but never completed. The land stayed nearly untouched until the 1960s. By 1926 the last section had been sold for a development of one-family houses. — https://brooklynreporter.com/nabetags/bergen-beach/ and https://brooklyneagle.com/2829/on-this-day-in-history-march-30-the-bergens-settle-in-brooklyn/
- The marshy land held back building. Large-scale residential development waited for fuller landfilling in the 1960s. Until then there was a small cluster of houses along Avenue U. — https://forgotten-ny.com/2008/11/bergen-beach-georgetown-brooklyn/
- Georgetown: Georgetown was developed in the 1960s. Per Forgotten New York, development ran from the 1960s through the 1980s, and some lots stayed empty until the 2000s. — https://brooklyneagle.com/303028/how-bergen-beach-got-its-name/ and https://forgotten-ny.com/2008/11/bergen-beach-georgetown-brooklyn/
- Sources conflict on the Georgetown timeline. Wikipedia says a 1960s "Georgetowne" development was proposed but never built, and Bergen Beach wasn't fully developed until the 1990s. Treat the exact dates cautiously. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bergen_Beach,_Brooklyn
- Bergen Beach became a popular residential area in the late 1980s and early 1990s. — https://brooklyneagle.com/303032/bergen-beach-history/

### C. Streets, corridors and geography
- Georgetowne is bounded by Ralph Avenue to the west, Avenue N to the south, Avenue U to the southeast, and Paerdegat Basin to the northeast. — https://www.nyc.gov/site/brooklyncb18/about/bergen-beach-georgetowne.page
- cityneighborhoods.nyc describes Bergen Beach as bounded by Avenue N, Paerdegat Basin, Mill Basin, East Mill Basin, 68th Street and Veterans Avenue. Sources define the boundaries differently. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/bergen-beach
- The neighborhood is in Brooklyn Community District 18, ZIP code 11234. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bergen_Beach,_Brooklyn
- Commercial corridors: Ralph Avenue (Georgetowne Shopping Center) and Avenue N. Italian-influenced businesses run along Avenues N and T. — https://italianenclaves.org/neighborhoods/east-coast/new-york/brooklyn/mill-basin-bergen-beach/
- Transit: There are no subways, only city buses. The Kings Highway station (Q and B) is about three miles away. Many residents rely on cars and the Belt Parkway. — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/brooklyn-ny/bergen-beach-neighborhood/

### D. Building stock summary
- Mostly one- and two-family detached and semi-detached houses, with some condos. Zoning is low-density residential (see above). No rowhouse, walk-up, co-op or high-rise stock was identified in the sources. — https://www.nyc.gov/site/brooklyncb18/about/bergen-beach-georgetowne.page
- The coastal neighborhood is mainly large family homes, some with private docks on East Mill Basin. — https://brooklyneagle.com/303032/bergen-beach-history/
- Market snapshot: the median sale price over the last 12 months was $792,000. — https://www.homes.com/brooklyn-ny/bergen-beach-neighborhood/sold/

### E. Parks, landmarks, and what it's known for
- Bergen Beach is surrounded by East Mill Basin, Paerdegat Basin and Jamaica Bay. Nearby Marine Park, with its nature areas, is a key point of interest. — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/brooklyn-ny/bergen-beach-neighborhood/ and https://www.bhsusa.com/neighborhood/new-york-city/brooklyn/bergen-beach
- Trulia's guide lists Paerdegat Basin Ecology Park and a PS 312 playground among nearby places. — https://www.trulia.com/n/ny/new-york/bergen-beach/85159/
- Known for a suburban feel: residents can sometimes be seen riding horses at the local stable. Isolation spared it from the 1970s fiscal-crisis blight. — https://brooklyneagle.com/303032/bergen-beach-history/ and https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/bergen-beach
- Official landmarks or NYC-designated landmark districts — EMPTY

### F. Security, entry systems and home technology
- Gated condo communities exist in Georgetown (see Section A). No local news on entry systems, intercoms, or smart-home adoption in Bergen Beach — EMPTY

### G. Recent local news and Reddit
- Local building-security news for Bergen Beach — EMPTY
- Reddit discussion — EMPTY
- Nextdoor posts mention vehicle theft, for example a car reported stolen on 9/7/25, but nothing about fire or building security. — https://nextdoor.com/neighborhood/bergenbeach1--brooklyn--ny/

### H. Anything that changes how installation is done there
- Beyond the points in Section A, the sources show no neighborhood-specific code or landmark constraints. NYC-wide FDNY and DOB rules still apply, but I did not verify them for this research.

## Bronx River, Bronx — /fire-alarm-installation-bronx-river-bronx-ny (factory research, 2026-10-27)

**Scope note:** "Bronx River" is not a formally bounded neighborhood. The sources place it in or beside Soundview, in Community District 9, ZIPs 10472 and 10473. One source describes it as the area on the east end of the river, between the Cross Bronx Expressway, Soundview Park on the south and the Bronx River Parkway on the east. CityNeighborhoods.NYC describes a different "Bronx River" with 2–3 story brick houses on Bronx Park East and Paulding Avenue. I did not use that description because it doesn't match the Soundview/Bronx River Houses area. Treat the geography below as a Soundview-based reading.

### 1. Fire-alarm-specific angles (most important)

- **Anchor building type: NYCHA high-rises.** Bronx River Houses is nine 14-story residential buildings with 1,247 apartments, completed February 28, 1951. Wikipedia gives 1,260 apartments, so the count varies by source. — https://citylore.org/places/bronx-river-houses/ and https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronx_River_Houses
- **Senior buildings next door.** The Bronx River Addition is two senior-only buildings, 6 and 14 stories tall, with 225 apartments. They were completed in 1966 and sit between E. 172nd and E. 174th Streets and Manor and Harrod Avenues. Senior occupancy is a fire-alarm design consideration. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronx_River_Houses
- **Local fatal fire at 1440 Bronx River Ave.** Firefighters found an e-bike in the 10th-floor apartment where the fire broke out at 1440 Bronx River Ave. A lithium-ion battery was the cause, and 78 firefighters from 20 units were needed. One person died. The page shows a 2026 publication stamp, but the aggregator ages it at about 1,000 days. Confirm the year before citing. — https://gothamist.com/news/lithium-ion-battery-caused-bronx-fire-that-killed-one-injured-several-others-fdny-says and https://www.fox5ny.com/news/bronx-apartment-fatal-fire-soundview
- **Cockloft fire and the smoke-alarm question, 1466 Watson Ave. (2019).** Firefighters found fire on the top floor in the cockloft space, between the ceiling and the roof. The FDNY investigated whether there were working smoke detectors in the building. A resident said the alarm didn't sound. This supports a page angle on detection in top-floor and cockloft spaces of older Soundview buildings. — https://abc7ny.com/four-alarm-fire-bronx-soundview-in-apartment-building/5390740/
- **That fire spread across buildings.** It affected at least two buildings after spreading through the cockloft. — https://ny1.com/nyc/all-boroughs/news/2019/07/12/fdny-responds-to-bronx-soundview-apartment-building-fire
- **High-rise fire, 825 Morrison Ave. (Jan 2026).** Flames broke out on the fourth floor of the 19-story building just after 5:30 p.m. Two people were hospitalized. — https://bronx.news12.com/2-people-hospitalized-one-with-serious-injuries-after-fire-erupts-in-soundview-apartment-building
- **High-rise fire, 1965 Lafayette Ave. (Dec 2025).** Crews responded to 1965 Lafayette Ave. in Soundview, where Citizen App video showed flames shooting out of upper-floor windows. The fire spread to three floors. — https://abc7ny.com/post/fire-burning-high-rise-apartment-building-castle-hill-bronx/18248211/
- **Soundview apartment fire, Sept 14, 2026.** It started on the fifth floor and injured three people, one critically. Firefighters had it under control just after 12:40 a.m. The building's address and the cause were not reported. — https://hoodline.com/2026/09/fifth-floor-soundview-fire-injures-three-one-critical-79-crews-respond/
- **Self-closing doors and lithium-ion context.** The same report notes FDNY fire marshals had investigated 282 lithium-ion battery fires citywide through August, versus 277 in all of 2024. It also says it is unresolved whether self-closing doors worked. Battery involvement in this fire is not established. — https://hoodline.com/2026/09/fifth-floor-soundview-fire-injures-three-one-critical-79-crews-respond/
- **Smaller fires on the street grid.** An April 2026 alert covers a 2nd-floor fire at 1035 Elder Ave. — https://newyork.liveuamap.com/en/2026/12-april-bronx-1075-box-2846-1035-elder-ave-soundview-fire-2nd-floor. A Citizen report covers a house-fire call at 1245 Stratford Ave., between E. 172nd St. and Westchester Ave. — https://citizen.com/-P0tuFtzk3S8r26l7AF9
- **Older Bruckner Blvd. fire.** An apartment fire on Bruckner Boulevard burst windows on the second and third floors and displaced three families. The article is undated and about 2,956 days old, so it is roughly 2018. — https://bronx.news12.com/officials-2-injured-after-apartment-building-fire-in-soundview-39153925
- **NYCHA fire alarm standards.** NYCHA design guidelines call for hard-wired carbon monoxide and smoke detectors with battery back-up, per NYC Building Code 907.2.11.2. NYCHA's procedure manual says the fire safety vendor inspects all alarms monthly and tests them semi-annually. In NYCHA buildings, new work generally goes through NYCHA's own capital and vendor process. — https://designguidelines.nycha.info/fire-protection/ and https://nychanow.nyc/wp/wp-content/uploads/2024/02/SP040043.pdf
- **Mitchell-Lama mid-rises (vendor claim, unverified).** An access-control vendor page says the 1970s Mitchell-Lama buildings on Soundview, Stratford, Watson and Commonwealth Avenues are 30–60 unit mid-rises with 50+ year old original lobby intercom systems. I could not verify this independently. Use it only as a lead, not as fact. — https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/access-control-installation-bronx-river-bronx-ny
- **NYC filing rules (general).** The FDNY fire alarm application requires FA plans (riser diagram, floor plans, I/O matrix) sealed and signed by the registered design professional. It also lists "Landmark Building/Flood Hazard Area compliances (if applicable)". The fire alarm plan requires a Licensed Master Electrician and S97 Holder. — https://nyc-business.nyc.gov/nycbusiness/description/fire-alarm-application
- **Mixed-use buildings (vendor claim).** One local vendor says ground-floor commercial occupancy needs a fire alarm control panel with pull stations and horn/strobes in addition to apartment-level detection. This is a vendor statement, not a code citation. — https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-riverdale-bronx-ny
- **Context from the 2022 Twin Parks fire.** Seventeen people died at 333 East 181st Street. The cause was an electrical space heater. This is a Bronx-wide reference point, not a local event. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Bronx_apartment_fire
- **HPD violations (smoke/CO detector complaints):** EMPTY. No neighborhood-level HPD data found.
- **311 complaints:** EMPTY.
- **DOB permits:** EMPTY.
- **LPC / landmark rules:** EMPTY. No landmark district was found for this area.
- **Reddit threads:** EMPTY. The search returned none.
- **Specific businesses and blocks where it matters:** EMPTY beyond the addresses above. No named businesses were found. Corridors where mixed-use fire alarm work would apply are listed in section 3.

### 2. History and development

- **River and name.** The river was called Aquehung, "River of High Bluffs," by the Mohegan; it attracted European traders, farmers and millers in the 1600s and 1700s. — https://www.nycgovparks.org/parks/X004/history
- **Industrial corridor.** The New York Central Railroad in the 1840s turned the valley into an industrial corridor. — https://www.nycgovparks.org/parks/X004/history
- **Parkway.** The Bronx River Parkway was completed in 1925. In the 1960s and 70s, highway projects distanced river communities from each other and from the river. — https://www.nyc.gov/site/dep/water/bronx-river.page
- **Soundview growth.** Soundview developed rapidly after the IRT Pelham Line was extended along Westchester Avenue in 1920, and large housing developments were built in the mid-20th century. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soundview,_Bronx
- **Temporary postwar housing.** In 1947, land was used for a temporary NYCHA project of 947 apartments in 473 Quonset huts. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soundview,_Bronx
- **Bronx River Houses.** The state-funded development was designed by William I. Hohauser, with landscape architect Leo A. Novick. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronx_River_Houses
- **Before the Houses.** The area was mostly a Jewish community before World War II; by the 1980s, Bronx River housed mostly Black and Latino residents. — https://citylore.org/places/bronx-river-houses/
- **Other NYCHA developments nearby.** Soundview Houses (1954) and Clason Point Gardens (1941) were built in the same era. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/soundview

### 3. Streets and corridors

- **Main corridors.** Westchester Avenue and Soundview Avenue are major commercial and transportation corridors; the Bruckner Expressway bisects the neighborhood. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soundview,_Bronx
- **Third corridor.** White Plains Road is also listed. The western border by the river is mostly storage, warehousing and automotive repair. Industrial and auto uses are relevant to commercial fire alarm scope. — https://bronxagents.com/neighborhood/soundview/
- **Bronx River Avenue.** The address 1440 Bronx River Ave. sits between E. 174th St. and the Cross Bronx Expressway. — https://www.fox5ny.com/news/bronx-apartment-fatal-fire-soundview
- **Auto parts and light industry.** The Greenway passes a former auto parts dealer's driveway at 1237 Bronx River Ave. — https://forgotten-ny.com/2026/10/bronx-river-starlight-part-2/

### 4. Building stock

- **Mix of types.** Soundview is more multifaceted than its public housing, with high-rises overlooking tree-shaded side streets. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/soundview
- **NYCHA scale (vendor-sourced list).** One vendor lists nine Soundview NYCHA developments, including Monroe Houses, Soundview Houses (13 buildings, 7 stories), Sack Wern Houses and Clason Point Gardens (45 buildings, 2 stories). — https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/access-control-installation-bronx-river-bronx-ny
- **Redevelopment on NYCHA land.** Soundview Housing is a $95 million, three-phase project creating 222 affordable units, awarded to Lemle & Wolff. New construction triggers full code-compliant fire alarm work. — https://soundviewhouses.us/about-us/
- **Total buildings.** OpenIgloo lists 2,428+ buildings in Soundview. It does not break them down by type. — https://www.openigloo.com/buildings/bronx/soundview
- **Landmark districts:** EMPTY.

### 5. Parks and landmarks

- **Concrete Plant Park (opened 2009).** It is about seven acres and part of the Bronx River Greenway. It was saved from the auction block in 1999 by residents led by Youth Ministries for Peace and Justice. — https://bronx.com/concrete-plant-park/
- **Starlight Park.** Originally an amusement park from the 1910s, it was renovated by 2013. Phase 2 includes 11 acres of parkland and three bridges. — https://davidwilliamrosales.com/2025/10/21/starlight-and-concrete-plant-park/ and https://bronxriver.org/restoration-access/developing-the-greenway/bridgethegapcompletestarlightparktofinishthebronxrivergreenway
- **Westchester Avenue station ruins.** Passenger trains stopped serving the NYNH&H station on the last day of 1937. The Greenway also passes the elevated #6 line on truss bridges at Westchester Avenue. — https://onfootonwheels.squarespace.com/other-walks-around-town/lower-bronx-river-greenway and https://forgotten-ny.com/2026/10/bronx-river-starlight-part-2/
- **Soundview Park.** It covers 205 acres in the southwestern part of the neighborhood. — https://bronxagents.com/neighborhood/soundview/

### 6. Known for

- **Hip-hop origins.** During the 1970s, Bronx River Houses became one of the central sites in the development of hip hop. Afrika Bambaataa grew up there, and his work developed out of the Bronx River Community Center. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronx_River_Houses and https://bronxriver.org/the-bronx-river-houses-resilience
- **Doo-wop.** Members of The Chiffons lived at Bronx River Houses and attended James Monroe High School. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronx_River_Houses
- **Greenway and river restoration.** See section 5.

### 7. Recent news relevant to security and entry

- **Shooting near Bronx River Ave. and E. 174th St.** A double shooting happened just after 11:45 p.m. on a Saturday, reported July 2026. It bears on after-hours access and lobby security. — https://bronx.news12.com/2026/07/29/gunman-on-the-run-after-double-shooting-in-soundview/2hDR182MkI1dJF06vL77wE
- **Home technology and entry systems:** EMPTY beyond the vendor intercom claim in section 1.
- **Reddit discussion:** EMPTY.

### 8. What would change how installation is done here

- **NYCHA buildings.** Work follows NYCHA's vendor and capital process and its guidelines. This is inferred from the NYCHA sources in section 1.
- **Older multi-story buildings.** Detection in cockloft and top-floor spaces matters, based on the 2019 Watson Ave. fire. A second angle is lithium-ion battery risk from e-bikes in apartments, based on 1440 Bronx River Ave.
- **Flood hazard area.** The FDNY application asks for flood-hazard compliance where applicable. Whether this applies near the river or Soundview waterfront is EMPTY. No flood-zone mapping was found.
- **Wireless versus hardwired for older stock.** Vendor pages say wireless suits older buildings where new cable would be destructive. They tie this to Grand Concourse co-ops, not to Soundview. Do not present it as a local finding. — https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-bronxwood-bronx-ny

## Brooklyn Chinatown, Brooklyn — /fire-alarm-installation-brooklyn-chinatown-brooklyn-ny (factory research, 2026-10-27)

Brooklyn Chinatown is Eighth Avenue in Sunset Park (Community Board 7). I found no source with fire-alarm data for the Chinatown strip itself, so many items below are Sunset Park-wide or citywide. Items marked EMPTY returned nothing usable.

### A. Fire alarm installation: local angles

**Building types and entry/wiring situations**
- A 1:30 a.m. fire on Nov. 15, 2025 broke out on the second floor of a mixed-use building at Eighth Avenue and 51st Street. It was a floor above the Chinese restaurant Bao House. Three civilians were injured. This is a clear example of the shop-below, apartments-above stock on the avenue. — https://brooklynreporter.com/2025/11/eighth-avenue-fire-injures-three/
- A two-alarm fire on Jan. 13, 2026 hit a four-story building at 512 48th St. and displaced 57 residents. The building had 15 open DOB violations at the time. News 12 says the violations were mainly boiler-related. — https://brooklyneagle.com/361717/57-residents-displaced-in-two-alarm-apartment-fire/ and https://brooklyn.news12.com/2-injured-in-2-alarm-fire-at-sunset-park-apartment-building
- At 702 54th Street, a three-alarm fire on May 2, 2022 started on the first floor. Tenants' complaint attributes it to lithium-ion batteries stored in an e-bike and scooter repair shop. Thirty-eight of the 40 rent-stabilized units' families were displaced. DOB records show three active violations directly related to the fire. — https://documentedny.com/2023/08/31/sunset-park-fire-legal-actiom/
- Cause is disputed for the 7th Avenue/54th Street fire. CBS reported e-bikes and scooters were recovered from a store at the base of the building. The same report says investigators did not believe the e-bikes or scooters were the cause. — https://www.cbsnews.com/newyork/news/fire-tears-through-apartment-building-in-sunset-park-brooklyn
- A fire in April 2019 at Seventh Avenue and 44th Street made 54 units uninhabitable. The Fire Department said an unattended candle in Apartment 6-B started it. The owners' engineer estimated reconstruction at $25 million. — https://www.thecityreporter.nyc/2020/11/16/brooklyn-sunset-park-condo-building-burned-fire/
- A 2011 arson in a four-story building at 61st Street, in Sunset Park's Chinatown area, injured 31 people. Fire marshals made the arrest after reviewing video surveillance tapes. — https://cbsnews.com/newyork/news/number-of-injuries-from-bklyn-fire-rises-to-40
- In March 2026, tenants said they had filed multiple 311 complaints about someone camping on donation bins, and one was marked closed hours before a fire. Neighbors and businesses also raised concerns about clothing donation bins near apartment entrances. The source doesn't give the exact address, so don't tie it to Eighth Avenue without checking. — https://hoodline.com/2026/03/sunset-park-families-flee-blaze-as-neighbors-blame-donation-bin-encampment/
- A four-alarm warehouse fire at 265 45th St. near Third Avenue involved a commercial warehouse with a lot of "materials." This is an example of the industrial and garment-loft stock at the edge of the neighborhood. — https://patch.com/new-york/sunset-park/fire-breaks-out-sunset-park-warehouse-fdny-says
- Old-building hazard from FDNY coverage of a Manhattan Chinatown fire (not Brooklyn). Cocklofts are open areas in older New York buildings between the top floor and the roof ceiling, and they are notorious fire-spreaders. Chief Gorman said a deck built over the cockloft made the fire labor-intensive to reach. — https://www.amny.com/news/firefighters-chinatown-two-alarm-fire-01142025/
- Social-media-only item, treat as unverified: a TikTok post says an e-bike caught fire inside a residential building on 8th Avenue. — https://www.tiktok.com/place/8th-Avenue-Chinatown,-Brooklyn,-NY-21568226349360436
- AV/intercom specifics for 8th Ave buildings (multilingual signage, door-entry audio/video): EMPTY.

**City data: HPD, 311, DOB**
- HPD violation counts vary widely by building. One Sunset Park building has 87 recorded HPD violations against a neighborhood average of 11.4. — https://augrented.com/nyc/3006760011-122-31-street
- At 225–229 23rd Street, tenants cited 318 HPD violations at a rally. That is not on Eighth Avenue, but it shows the enforcement climate. — https://www.brownstoner.com/real-estate-market/sunset-park-23rd-street-tenants-association-protest/
- 311 routes most housing complaints to HPD, which handles heat, leaks, mold and similar issues. Violations should appear on the HPD website within about a week. — https://www.justfix.org/en/learn/guide-to-calling-311/
- Illegal conversions: DOB defines an illegal conversion as altering a building to create extra units without DOB permits or approval. DOB says these conversions pose serious safety risks to tenants and first responders. City campaign flyers stress two exits, working smoke alarms and CO detectors. — https://www.nyc.gov/site/buildings/tenant/illegal-conversion.page and https://home4.nyc.gov/site/buildings/dob/pr-illegal-apartments.page
- DOB, FDNY and HPD's 2016 flyer campaign targeted 53rd Street in Bay Ridge, a few blocks south of the Chinatown stretch, and Grand Street in Manhattan's Chinatown. — https://home4.nyc.gov/site/buildings/dob/pr-illegal-conversion-danger-warning.page
- Citywide figures for Eighth Avenue specifically (311 counts, DOB fire-alarm permit counts): EMPTY.

**Permits and rules that shape the work**
- An FDNY fire alarm plan requires a licensed Master Electrician and S97 holder. Applicants need a Certificate of Occupancy or an approved Schedule A as proof of occupancy. They also need floor plans, a riser diagram and an operating sequence matrix, plus landmark compliance documents if needed. The system must be 100% complete and pre-tested before the Fire Alarm Inspection Unit inspects. — https://nyc-business.nyc.gov/nycbusiness/description/fire-alarm-application
- DOB files fire alarm work as a separate Alteration-2 application with an "FA" work type. DOB signs off once the FDNY approval letter and as-built plans arrive. This source is an older DOB course deck, so check current procedure. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/buildings/pdf/filing_rep_course_207.pdf
- During renovation work, DOB policy lets smoke detectors be covered or removed. It generally requires other components, including sprinkler flow alarms and manual pull stations, to stay operational. An impairment coordinator must be designated. — https://www.ccny.cuny.edu/sites/default/files/NYC%20Fire%20Code%20Guide.pdf
- FDNY rules allow professional certification of a completed, tested installation by a certificate-of-fitness holder, filed electronically. — https://rules.cityofnewyork.us/wp-content/uploads/2022/08/fire-alarm-rule-amendments-104-02-104-04-105-01-3-16-23-revised-promulgation-final.pdf
- The 2014 Fire Code took effect March 30, 2014. It was the first periodic revision of the 2008 Fire Code, which took effect July 1, 2008. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/fdny/downloads/pdf/business/nyc-fire-code-guide.pdf
- Places of assembly require a Fire Department permit and a DOB certificate of operation. The Fire Department recognizes that fire alarm systems now required in many such places enhance their safety. This is relevant to banquet halls and restaurants on the avenue. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/fdny/downloads/pdf/business/nyc-fire-code-guide.pdf
- Pre-1968 code text: catering establishments and banquet halls with an occupant load of 300 or more are listed for sprinklers. Older fire alarm systems installed under earlier rules are accepted as long as they are maintained in good working order. — https://www.nyc.gov/html/dob/downloads/bldgs_code/bc27s17.pdf and https://nycadmincode.readthedocs.io/t27/c01/sch17/art05/
- LPC rules: LPC designated four Sunset Park historic districts on June 18, 2019: North, South, Central and 50th Street. Sunset Park South lies between Fourth and Fifth Avenues and 54th–59th Streets. The 50th Street district frames 50 row houses between Fourth and Fifth Avenues. Whether any Eighth Avenue lot falls inside a district: EMPTY. Check each address with LPC before drilling or running conduit in a façade. — https://www.citylandnyc.org/landmarks-designates-sunset-park-historic-districts/
- Local Reddit threads about fire alarms or smoke detectors in Brooklyn Chinatown: EMPTY. The searches returned no Reddit results.

### B. Neighborhood background

**History and development**
- In the late 1970s Sunset Park was predominantly Puerto Rican and white. More Chinese people began settling in the 1980s, with only a few thousand around Eighth Avenue. — https://www.bklynlibrary.org/podcasts/building-brooklyn-eighth
- Eighth Avenue was once "Little Norway" or "Lapskaus Boulevard," and it declined after the Norwegians left by the 1950s. The first Chinese grocery in Brooklyn opened in 1983, a Cantonese seafood restaurant in 1985 and Winley Supermarket in 1986. Wikipedia carries some uncited claims, so treat it as secondary. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatowns_in_Brooklyn
- By 1988 there were about 3,000 Asian-Americans and 12 Asian-American-operated businesses in Sunset Park. Property was inexpensive and space was readily available. The Eighth Avenue N station was nicknamed the "blue sky" station. — https://www.bca.net/eng/about.html
- The population has shifted from Cantonese to heavily Fujianese since 2000. — https://atlasofhome.com/roots/chinese-in-sunset-park-brooklyn/
- An 1889 ferry service at Second Avenue and 39th Street stimulated growth. The first immigrants were Irish, German and Scandinavian, and large swaths of rowhouses were built for them. — https://6tocelebrate.org/neighborhood-items/sunset-park-brooklyn/
- Irving T. Bush founded the Bush Terminal Company in 1895. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/sunset-park

**Main streets and corridors**
- The Chinese commercial economy runs along Eighth Avenue between 39th and 65th Streets. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eighth_Avenue_(Brooklyn)
- 5th Avenue has many Latin restaurants and shops. — https://www.bkreader.com/neighborhood/neighbhorhood-profile-sunset-park-8566308
- The Eighth Avenue N station is at 62nd Street. — https://atlasofhome.com/roots/chinese-in-sunset-park-brooklyn/

**Building stock**
- Sunset Park has one of the city's earliest and most extensive concentrations of two-family masonry rowhouses, mostly built 1885–1912. It also has multiple-family dwellings, tenements and early non-profit cooperative apartment buildings. — https://6tocelebrate.org/neighborhood-items/sunset-park-brooklyn/
- After 1910, larger apartment buildings, flats and tenements were built. The subway's arrival (1912–13) pushed larger multi-family building. One example row of three-story tenements has two apartments per floor. — https://www.citylandnyc.org/landmarks-designates-sunset-park-historic-districts/ and https://6tocelebrate.org/neighborhoods/sunset-park/
- Prewar six-story walk-ups and elevator buildings from the 1920s–30s cluster near Fort Hamilton Parkway and Fourth Avenue. This is a real-estate broker source. — https://reinventny.com/blog/sunset-park
- The Dr. Maurice T. Lewis House is a freestanding mansion built in 1907. — https://kids.kiddle.co/Sunset_Park,_Brooklyn
- The cockloft/old-building comparison above EMPTY for Eighth Avenue specifically: no source describes the avenue's storefront-building eras, heights or construction type.

**Parks and landmarks**
- Sunset Park itself is a 24-acre hilltop park laid out in the 1890s. Other sources say 25 acres. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/sunset-park
- The Sunset Play Center is an exterior and interior NYC landmark. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunset_Park_(Brooklyn_park)
- The Brooklyn Army Terminal was built near the end of World War I and designed by Cass Gilbert. — https://www.untappedcities.com/secrets-sunset-park-brooklyn/
- Bush Terminal was renamed Industry City in the mid-1980s. A 3,237-building area was listed on the National Register of Historic Places in 1988. — https://6tocelebrate.org/neighborhood-items/sunset-park-brooklyn/
- The 228th Engine Company fire station, built in 1891, is a landmark. — https://kids.kiddle.co/Sunset_Park,_Brooklyn

**Known for**
- Brooklyn's "Chinatown South," with Chinese immigrants from Manhattan's Chinatown and Fujian Province and its supermarkets, dim sum restaurants and herbal shops. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/sunset-park
- Garment factories on the industrial outskirts were a major employer. — https://www.bca.net/eng/about.html

**Recent news**
- On Oct. 23, 2026, officials and about 10,000 community members gathered at 60th Street and 8th Avenue to co-name the street "Brooklyn Chinatown." Council Member Susan Zhuang said public safety has improved. — https://www.bkreader.com/lifestyle-culture/brooklyn-chinatown-officially-co-named-in-sunset-park-12938645 and https://asamnews.com/2026/10/26/brooklyn-chinatown-street-co-naming-eighth-avenue-sunset-park/
- Security and entry-system news specific to Eighth Avenue (break-ins, package theft, intercom upgrades): EMPTY.

**Reddit**
- No Brooklyn Chinatown or Sunset Park threads surfaced. EMPTY.

**Businesses and blocks where fire alarms matter (from the sources above)**
- Eighth Avenue and 51st Street: mixed-use building with the Bao House restaurant. — https://brooklynreporter.com/2025/11/eighth-avenue-fire-injures-three/
- 5517–5523 Eighth Avenue area: Winley Supermarket at 5523 8th Ave, plus the first seafood restaurant between 55th and 56th Streets (history only). — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatowns_in_Brooklyn
- The Brooklyn Chinese-American Association is at the 5000 Eighth Avenue area, per a secondary source. — https://atlasofhome.com/roots/chinese-in-sunset-park-brooklyn/
- 7th Avenue at 44th and 54th Streets, and 48th Street between 5th and 6th Avenues: recent fire sites, covered above.

## Brooklyn Navy Yard, Brooklyn — /fire-alarm-installation-brooklyn-navy-yard-brooklyn-ny (factory research, 2026-10-27)

I ran out of searches before covering city-data and Reddit angles. Those items are marked EMPTY below.

### A. Fire alarm installation: local angles

**Building types and entry/wiring/AV situations**
- **Building 77 (141 Flushing Ave):** The Navy built it in 1942 as a windowless storage warehouse, and the Navy Yard spent $187 million upgrading it. A leasing flyer lists a $185 million capital improvement plan completed in 2017, new HVAC and plumbing, and 8 passenger elevators. The sources give two cost figures ($187M and $185M). — https://commercialobserver.com/2021/06/the-brooklyn-navy-yard-wants-to-appeal-to-creative-office-tenants/ and https://www.brooklynnavyyard.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/BNY-BLDG-77_2023-Flyer-Final-507-Flyer.pdf
- **Building 77 life safety:** The leasing flyer's life-safety line reads "Fully sprinklered by new combined sprinkler/standpipe" and then mentions a new "addressable" system. The snippet cuts off, so I can't confirm it is the fire alarm panel. — https://www.brooklynnavyyard.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/BLDG-77-Suite-106.pdf
- **Building 77 access:** Security is a lobby desk with 24/7 security and keycard access. The ground floor is accessible to the public, and roughly 11K+ Navy Yard tenant employees have access to the food hall daily. Alarm work there has to cope with public traffic and keycard-controlled tenant floors. — https://www.brooklynnavyyard.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/BLDG-77-Suite-106.pdf
- **Building 77 top floors:** The 15th floor has four freight elevators and four private outdoor terraces. The 16th floor has about 8,000 sq ft of interior space and 16,500 sq ft of roof space, suited to a restaurant or event use. That means assembly-type occupancies on the roof. — https://brooklyneagle.com/106192/brooklyn-navy-yard-signs-leasing-agreement-with-cbre-for-top-floor-waterfront-spaces/
- **Building 77 food hall:** The renovated ground floor has a food hall with Russ & Daughters, Transmitter Brewing and The Food Sermon. Commercial kitchens bring their own detection and suppression-interface needs. — https://commercialobserver.com/2021/06/the-brooklyn-navy-yard-wants-to-appeal-to-creative-office-tenants/
- **Dock 72:** It is a 675,000 sq ft building. Sources disagree on height: 16 stories (Architect's Newspaper) versus 17 stories (Metropolis). It has dual cores and two entrances, one from the land side and one from the water side. The building has an app for expedited security. — https://www.dock72.com/the-building/ and https://www.archpaper.com/2017/10/wework-brooklyn-navy-yard-tops-out/
- **Dock 72 lobby:** The lobby is 30 feet wide and 600 feet long. The ground floor is supported by 20 V-shaped steel beams. The long lobby and exposed structure affect device layout. — https://metropolismag.com/projects/dock-72-interior-design/
- **Dock 72 ferry gate:** The city's Design Commission approved electrical equipment, fencing, a security booth and a turnstile enclosure for citywide ferry service at Dock 72 (October 2017). — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/designcommission/downloads/pdf/meeting-minutes-10-16-2017-.pdf
- **Building 12 (industrial):** Features include 20' ceiling heights, a mezzanine storage level, 3 gantry cranes, 6 drive-in gates and workshop spaces. Security is 24/7 Navy Yard security. High ceilings and shop environments point to heat, beam or air-sampling detection rather than plain spot smoke detectors. That is my inference. — https://www.brooklynnavyyard.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/02/BNY-BLDG-12_2024-Flyer.pdf
- **Building 303:** Built in 2020, by Dattner Architects, next to the Sands gate. The Yard markets its top floors as traditional office space. — https://www.brooklynnavyyard.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/02/BNY-BLDG-303_2024-Flyer-Suite-901-1.pdf
- **Admiral's Row development:** The redevelopment includes 360,000 sq ft of light industrial and office space and 165,000 sq ft of retail. Wegmans has a 74,000 sq ft store there. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admiral%27s_Row and https://www.6sqft.com/new-renderings-of-wegmans-building-kick-off-leasing-at-brooklyn-navy-yard-admirals-row/
- **Perimeter and access control:** BNYDC advertises around-the-clock security patrol, 24-hour accessibility and a secured perimeter. The Yard has four secure Yard entrances. General public access is restricted. The Yard separates public-facing spaces, guided-tour areas and secured working zones. Installers should expect gate check-in and escort coordination. — https://www.idealist.org/en/nonprofit/89914ba8abb84ea0aaddb9aa48c44a0e-brooklyn-navy-yard-development-organization-new-york and https://www.newyorkknow.com/blogs/brooklyn-navy-yard
- **Ownership and who to coordinate with:** BNYDC is the not-for-profit that serves as real estate developer and property manager on behalf of the City of New York. Work in tenant spaces likely involves BNYDC approvals. That is my inference. — https://a856-cityrecord.nyc.gov/RequestDetail/20170726004
- **Older-building hazard:** A mesothelioma-awareness site says asbestos was used as insulation in walls, boilers, incinerators and around pipes at the Navy Yard. This is a secondary source, but it is a reason for an asbestos survey before drilling or cable pulls in pre-1966 buildings. — https://www.mesothelioma.com/asbestos-exposure/jobsites/shipyards/brooklyn-navy-yard/

**Fire-related local news and history**
- **Warehouse fire:** A fire gutted a warehouse at the Navy Yard and injured 67 firefighters. It began when thousands of powder-free latex gloves spontaneously combusted. The snippet gives no date. — https://www.tjrusso.com/brooklyn-navy-yard-fire
- **USS Constellation fire:** A forklift pierced a fuel tank during construction, and the fuel ignited. The fire killed 50 people and injured 323. — https://www.brooklynnavyyard.org/history/
- **FDNY fireboats:** FDNY's Marine Operations Division and its fireboats are at Building 292. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn_Navy_Yard
- **Manhole fire:** Citizen logged a manhole fire at Hudson Ave & Front St, Navy Yard, published Jul 2. The year isn't stated in the snippet. — https://citizen.com/-Ow_U7bujJNrL97bY2RH
- **Large-event crowds:** On Aug 31, 2026, Councilmembers Lincoln Restler and Crystal Hudson wrote to the BNYDC CEO about all-night raves in the Yard's dance warehouses. The Navy Yard added an event hotline and changed exit procedures. Assembly-occupancy egress and alarm coverage in warehouse venues is a possible angle. — https://brooklyneagle.com/412568/neighbors-fed-up-with-loud-all-night-raves-at-brooklyn-navy-yard/
- **Simulated explosion:** A Notify NYC alert covered a simulated explosion with a 20-foot fireball near Washington Ave and Flushing Ave by the Navy Yard. The article is dated Oct 21, 2026. — https://www.yahoo.com/news/articles/don-t-freak-see-fireball-132503679.html

**City data and Reddit**
- HPD violations: EMPTY
- 311 complaints: EMPTY
- DOB permits: EMPTY
- Reddit threads: EMPTY
- Rules for NYC-designated landmarks (LPC): EMPTY. Dry Dock 1 and Quarters A are city landmarks (see section C), but I found no LPC rules about fire alarm work. The Yard is city-owned, and the city's Design Commission has reviewed exterior work there. See the Building 92 courtyard and Dock 72 ferry gate items. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/designcommission/downloads/pdf/meeting-minutes-10-16-2017-.pdf

**Caution on a competitor page**
- The Abstract Enterprises page for this keyword says the company is headquartered in Brooklyn Navy Yard at 1282 Troy Ave. The Navy Yard is bounded by Navy Street, Flushing Avenue, Kent Avenue and the East River. I did not verify where Troy Ave is, so don't repeat that claim. — https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-brooklyn-navy-yard-brooklyn-ny

### B. History and development
- **Founding:** President John Adams authorized the first five naval shipyards, including Brooklyn's, in 1801. Quarters A, completed in 1806, is the oldest building associated with the Yard. — https://www.brooklynnavyyard.org/history/
- **Land purchase:** The federal government bought forty acres of marsh and meadow on Wallabout Bay from the Remsen, Ryerson and Vanderbilt families. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/brooklyn-navy-yard
- **Before 1801:** Dutch settler Jansen de Rapelje bought 335 acres on Wallabout Bay from the Lenape in 1637. As many as 11,500 died on British prison ships off Wallabout Bay. — https://www.brooklynnavyyard.org/history/
- **Ships built:** The Yard launched the USS Monitor, USS Arizona and USS Missouri. About 70,000 people worked there in WWII. It ran continuously until 1966, when it was decommissioned and sold to the City. — https://www.brooklynnavyyard.org/history/
- **Closure and reopening:** The Yard shut in 1966 and reopened in 1971 when New York City bought it for redevelopment. Defense Secretary McNamara closed it, and over 9,000 employees had to look for work elsewhere. — https://www.walmart.com/ip/11550595 and https://theclio.com/entry/19603
- **Today:** Expansion since 2017 included Building 77 and Dock 72, and employment doubled by 2020. — https://citydays.com/places/brooklyn-navy-yard/
- **Scale and tenants:** BNYDC cites over 40 buildings, 3 NYC landmarks, 4 million sq ft of leasable space, over 330 tenants and more than 7,000 workers. Acreage varies by source: 225.15 acres for the National Register and roughly 300 acres in marketing. — https://www.idealist.org/en/nonprofit/89914ba8abb84ea0aaddb9aa48c44a0e-brooklyn-navy-yard-development-organization-new-york
- **Film studios:** The Yard hosts the largest film studios on the East Coast. — same Idealist URL.

### C. Streets, corridors and surrounding neighborhoods
- **Boundaries:** Navy Street to the west, Flushing Avenue to the south, Kent Avenue to the east and the East River to the north. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn_Navy_Yard
- **Neighbors:** Williamsburg, Fort Greene, Clinton Hill and DUMBO border it. — https://citylore.org/places/brooklyn-navy-yard/
- **Flushing Ave:** Building 77 (141 Flushing Ave) and Building 92 (63 Flushing Ave, at Carlton Ave) front it. — https://www.hmdb.org/m.asp?m=95831
- **Sands Street gate:** Construction started in 1895 and the gate opened a year later. Since 2015 it has housed the Kings County Distillery tasting room. — https://kids.kiddle.co/Brooklyn_Navy_Yard
- **Sands Street history:** It was once a lively row of entertainments serving Navy Yard sailors and workers. Most of that was cleared when the Farragut Houses were built in the late 1940s. — https://forgotten-ny.com/2009/11/vinegar-hill-part-2-brooklyn/
- **Historic fire risk:** A 1930s-era guide described ramshackle frame houses north of Sands Street as "notorious firetraps." This is historical colour only. — https://www.brooklyn.net/neighborhoods/navy_yard_01.html
- **Transit:** The A/C to High Street–Brooklyn Bridge, the F to York Street, or the G to Clinton/Washington. — https://ww2.aaa.com/tripcanvas/attraction/brooklyn-navy-yard-VA9791
- **Wallabout:** It is bounded by Navy Street, the Navy Yard and Flushing Avenue, Myrtle Avenue and Marcy Avenue. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wallabout,_Brooklyn
- **Vinegar Hill (adjacent residential):** Mostly 19th-century Federal and Greek Revival homes mixed with industrial buildings. Hudson Avenue and Plymouth, Water and Front Streets are Belgian block. It includes NYCHA's Farragut Houses. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinegar_Hill,_Brooklyn

### D. Building stock and landmarks
- **Architectural range:** More than 45 buildings, from the early 19th to the 21st century, in Greek Revival, Italianate, Second Empire, Art Deco and Moderne styles. — https://citylore.org/places/brooklyn-navy-yard/
- **Historic district:** The Brooklyn Navy Yard Historic District was added to the National Register on May 22, 2014, and designated on the State Register on March 31, 2014. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn_Navy_Yard
- **NYC landmarks:** Dry Dock 1, completed in 1851, is a New York City designated landmark. The Navy Yard Hospital Building and Surg

## Cadman Plaza, Brooklyn — /fire-alarm-installation-cadman-plaza-brooklyn-ny (factory research, 2026-10-27)

**Note on scope:** Cadman Plaza is a park and street pair, not a formal neighborhood. It sits on the border of Brooklyn Heights and Downtown Brooklyn. Several findings below are filed under "Brooklyn Heights" for that reason.

### A. Fire alarm installation: local angles (most important)

- The co-op at 140 Cadman Plaza West is a Mitchell-Lama development known as Cadman Plaza North. It is the northernmost of three Robert Moses-era urban renewal projects, collectively the Cadman Plaza Development. A fire there in December 2022 was on the ninth floor and injured two people, with "minor injuries." — https://brooklyneagle.com/articles/2022/12/31/fire-at-brooklyn-heights-high-rise-injures-two/ and http://brooklynheightsblog.com/archives/96493
- A smoky fire on the fourth floor of 75 Henry St. spread acrid smoke through the 32-story co-op, and worried residents went into the hallways. Tenants near the affected apartment were alerted through the building intercom by the doorman. The unit had been under renovation for six months, with construction materials inside. Angle: alarm notification in older Mitchell-Lama and co-op towers, and fire risk during renovations. — https://brooklyneagle.com/articles/2022/03/06/smoky-fire-breaks-out-in-brooklyn-heights-highrise-panicking-residents
- On July 28, 2025, a smoky fire broke out on the ninth floor of a 15-story office tower at 185 Montague St., between Cadman Plaza West and Clinton Street. The main stairwell led to a lobby full of smoke, and the safety stairwell opened onto the sidewalk next to a Chipotle. 26 units and 84 FDNY and EMS personnel responded. A medical-office tenant said "We smelled it and then the alarm went off." Angle: commercial and medical offices in the Montague Street high-rise corridor. — https://brooklyneagle.com/articles/2025/07/28/smoky-fire-breaks-out-at-brooklyn-heights-office-building
- A five-alarm fire broke out in a mixed-use apartment building on Montague Street, at the corner of Montague and Henry. It started on the second floor and spread upward, and the building and the two on either side were evacuated. The article's date isn't shown in the result. A neighbor said a woman upstairs smelled smoke and knocked on his door. Angle: mixed-use buildings with ground-floor retail under apartments. — https://www.cbsnews.com/newyork/news/stubborn-blaze-tears-through-brooklyn-heights-building/
- On Aug. 31, 2026, the Eagle reported that FDNY received a call of a "transformer fire affecting occupancies with smoke, CO and power issues." The fire popped a manhole cover and caused a dangerous buildup of carbon monoxide gas in nearby homes. Emergency vehicles lined Middagh and Henry streets all the way to Cadman Plaza West. Angle: CO detection alongside smoke detection. — https://brooklyneagle.com/412500/transformer-fire-causes-carbon-monoxide-buildup-evacuations-in-brooklyn-heights/
- In May 2023, FDNY rushed to the Brooklyn War Memorial in Cadman Plaza Park after smoke was seen pouring from the roof. The building also contains NYC Parks Department and Cadman Park Conservancy offices. Angle: a public monument-and-office building inside the park. — https://brooklyneagle.com/articles/2023/05/03/brooklyn-war-memorial-in-cadman-plaza-park-catches-fire/
- The one-story Park Plaza Diner on Cadman Plaza West, across the park from the Federal Courthouse, was evacuated when a fire broke out on its roof. It is a frequent lunch spot for lawyers, reporters and news crews. The article's date isn't shown; its age metadata suggests about 2012. Angle: restaurants near the courthouses. — https://brooklyneagle.com/6011/brooklyn-diner-frequented-by-lawyers-and-defendants-alike-closed-by-fire/
- A job posting seeks a part-time Fire & Life Safety Director at 271 Cadman Plaza East. The role covers FDNY and building-plan compliance, required drills and training, and inspection-ready documentation. This shows a large commercial building on the street with an FLSD program. — https://www.dreamworkhq.com/job/1fce46b8-4caa-483f-b8ea-0caaecbf9711
- Historic fire context: the 1876 Brooklyn Theatre fire took place on the site of what is now 271 Cadman Plaza. It killed at least 278 people. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn_Theatre_fire
- Nearby FDNY: Engine Company 205/Ladder Company 118 is at 74 Middagh Street, and Engine Company 224 is at 274 Hicks Street. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn_Heights
- **New construction:** Demolition wrapped at 205 Montague Street by late June, and by July work had started on a 47-story tower designed by Hill West Architects. The tower is 672 feet tall, with 90 rental apartments and 46 condominium units. It also has nearly 40,000 square feet of retail and more than 100 enclosed parking spots. The Serhant piece doesn't give a year for the demolition, but it is dated roughly late September 2026. Angle: a new high-rise needing a full high-rise fire alarm design with FDNY filing. — https://serhant.com/new/blog/the-year-brooklyn-heights-finally-got-its-answers and https://hoodline.com/2026/04/skyline-shock-as-47-story-montague-street-tower-muscles-into-brooklyn-heights/
- One Clinton at 280 Cadman Plaza West opened in 2021 as a 38-story luxury condo. It has a Sky Lounge, a fitness center and a public library branch at its base. Sources conflict on height: Wikipedia lists 36 floors and 409 feet. It replaced a preexisting Brooklyn Public Library branch. Angle: a mixed condo and public library building. — https://www.cityrealty.com/nyc/brooklyn-heights/one-clinton-280-cadman-plaza-west/68115 and https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1_Clinton_Street
- Cadman Towers (101 Clark St.) is a co-op. A January 2024 article said its roughly 50-year-old pipes from the street into the building are the co-op's responsibility. A water main break flooded its basement mechanical room. Angle: aging infrastructure and basement mechanical and panel rooms in the 1960s complex. — https://brooklyneagle.com/articles/2024/01/23/burst-water-pipe-causes-havoc-at-brooklyn-heights-co-op/
- Today's date (Oct. 27, 2026) fits a drill: a Community Emergency Response Team fire safety exercise runs Oct. 27–28, 6:30–9:30 p.m., around Cadman Plaza East and Red Cross Place, per a Notify NYC alert. — https://brooklyneagle.com/whats-news-breaking/
- **Code framework (citywide, not Cadman-specific):**
  - BC 907 requires owners to submit fire alarm documents to the FDNY for approval before installation.
  - FC 907 takes over after installation.
  - Meeting NFPA 72 alone does not satisfy NYC's requirements.
  - Source: https://www.tfp1.com/blog/fdny-fire-alarm-inspection-requirements/
- A residential occupancy needs a fire alarm when dwelling units are at least three stories above the lowest exit discharge level, or the building has at least 16 dwelling units. A vendor blog gives these conditions and says it's a general guide, not a replacement for the code. — https://www.ny-engineers.com/blog/fire-alarm-device-requirements-by-occupancy-classification
- Control panel replacement or relocation requires new plans filed with FDNY. A vendor source also says alterations may require upgrading to the 2022 Code and, in some cases, ARCS must be installed. — https://asmintegrators.com/fire-alarm-code/
- FDNY guidance says landlords must install smoke alarms outside each sleeping room, within 15 feet of the room entrance. It also says landlords must install carbon monoxide alarms in the same locations as smoke alarms. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/fdny/downloads/pdf/business/Support/smoke-carbon-monoxide-alarms-english.pdf
- Competitor page caution: Abstract Enterprises has a templated "Cadman Plaza" page. It lists industrial zones such as Sunset Park, East New York, Red Hook and the Brooklyn Navy Yard as Cadman Plaza's. It also gives its address as 1282 Troy Ave, which is not in Cadman Plaza. Treat its claims as unreliable. — https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-cadman-plaza-brooklyn-ny
- HPD violations data for Cadman Plaza buildings — EMPTY (not found)
- 311 complaints data — EMPTY (not found)
- DOB permit data for fire alarms (beyond the 205 Montague permits) — EMPTY (not found)
- Reddit threads about fire alarms or buildings at Cadman Plaza — EMPTY (searches returned none)
- Specific AV, intercom or entry-system details for these buildings — EMPTY, apart from the intercom alert at 75 Henry above

### B. LPC and landmarks rules affecting installation

- The Brooklyn Heights Historic District runs from Cadman Plaza West (Old Fulton Street) on the north to the BQE on the west, Atlantic Avenue on the south, and an irregular line partly along Clinton and Henry Streets on the east. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn_Heights_Historic_District
- The LPC reviews changes to exterior features visible from a public way, such as a street, sidewalk or park. Interior wiring is not mentioned in this source. DOB issues building permits after the appropriate LPC approval is in place. Visible exterior devices such as strobes and horns on landmarked facades would plausibly trigger review. That is an inference, not stated in the source. — https://brennanglobalproperties.com/blog/what-the-brooklyn-heights-historic-district-means-for-owners
- The LPC created the Borough Hall Skyscraper District in 2011, protecting 21 buildings near Borough Hall. The north side of Montague between Cadman Plaza West and Clinton Street was excluded when the district was designated. — https://thebha.org/issue/bha-landmarks-committee-activity/
- The Cadman Plaza urban renewal site (Pineapple Walk and 75 Henry) was described as outside the district, with the LPC having no control or review right over the site. This comes from a blog commenter, not an official source. — http://brooklynheightsblog.com/archives/77759
- The Brooklyn City Railroad Company Building at 8 Cadman Plaza West is a designated individual landmark. — https://hdc.org/borough/brooklyn-heights/

### C. History and development

- Downtown Brooklyn and Brooklyn Heights remained sparsely inhabited until 1814, when Robert Fulton's steam ferry began. The Sands Street Terminal elevated station was completed in 1883 in the area now occupied by Cadman Plaza. — https://www.cadmanpark.org/park-history
- The bulk of the ten-square-block property was condemned in 1935 and named Cadman Plaza in 1939. The Fulton Street El was demolished in 1941, and the Sands Street terminal was razed in 1943. — https://www.nycgovparks.org/parks/cadman-plaza-park-and-brooklyn-war-memorial/history
- Moses era: the park reflected Parks Commissioner Robert Moses's goals of more parkland and better traffic circulation. The area became the largest post-WWII civic center urban renewal project in the country. The entire Cadman project was completed before the decade was out. — https://www.tclf.org/cadman-plaza-park, https://www.brownstoner.com/history/past-and-present-cadman-plaza/ and https://www.brownstoner.com/history/past-and-present-aerial-view-of-cadman-plaza/
- Residential complex: Cadman Plaza North (140 Cadman Plaza West), Whitman Close (75 Henry St.) and Cadman Towers (101 Clark St. and 10 Clinton St.) were built in the late 1960s where the Brooklyn Bridge trolley terminal once stood. They were funded through the Mitchell-Lama program. — https://findingaids.library.nyu.edu/cbh/arc_181_cadman_plaza_complex/
- The Brooklyn Rail says about 1,200 residents, mostly low-income minorities, small businesses and sound 20th-century apartment houses were declared a blight and demolished. This is a first-person essay. — https://brooklynrail.org/2015/07/field-notes/brooklyn-heights-blows-it-the-untold-story-of-cadman-plaza/

### D. Streets, corridors, building stock, landmarks

- The plaza is bounded by Cadman Plaza East (formerly Washington Street) and Cadman Plaza West (formerly Ferry Road or Fulton Road). The Brooklyn Bridge is on the north and Tillary Street on the south. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cadman_Plaza
- Montague Street is Brooklyn Heights' main shopping street, with stores and restaurants also on Henry Street. — https://thebha.org/neighborhood/
- Brooklyn Heights housing includes 19th-century Greek Revival, Federal, Italianate and Romanesque Revival homes. A Wikipedia snippet notes Cadman Towers HDFC, Cadman Plaza North (Mitchell-Lama) and the Concord Village co-op development on Adams Street. — https://thekanthateam.com/blog/the-difference-between-a-rowhouse-brownstone-and-townhouse-in-nyc and https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn_Heights
- Cadman Plaza North: built in 1967, 350 units, 27 stories. — https://www.apartments.com/cadman-plaza-north-brooklyn-ny/rzyv3h1/
- Park: 10.4 acres, originally Brooklyn Bridge Plaza. The Brooklyn War Memorial was dedicated in 1952. A monument honors Mayor William Jay Gaynor on the west side. — https://www.tclf.org/cadman-plaza-park
- Civic buildings: the Theodore Roosevelt US Courthouse at 225 Cadman Plaza East was completed in 2006 and designed by César Pelli, with 15 floors. The Kings County Supreme Court Building and Columbus Park are south of Johnson. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodore_Roosevelt_United_States_Courthouse and https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cadman_Plaza
- Known for: Brooklyn Heights became a National Historic Landmark and the city's first Historic District in 1965. The park has long been a place of political action. A site on Montague held the Brooklyn Dodgers' headquarters, where Jackie Robinson was signed. A plaque is on 205 Montague. — https://thebha.org/neighborhood/, https://pulsd.com/new-york/institutions/cadman-plaza-park and http://brooklynheightsblog.com/archives/99323
