## Claremont, Bronx — /fire-alarm-installation-claremont-bronx-ny (factory research, 2026-10-27)

**Limits of this research:** I could not find any Reddit thread, LPC landmark-district rule or DOB permit record for Claremont, so those are marked EMPTY below. The HPD and 311 figures come from a third-party aggregator (augrented.com) that rolls up city data. They are not the city portals themselves. The data should be re-pulled from NYC Open Data before publishing.

### 1. Fire alarm facts specific to Claremont

**Recent fires (the strongest local angles)**
- A five-alarm fire broke out on Findlay Avenue on Jan 5, 2026, at a first-floor business (Deli Grocery Inc.) in a mixed-use building between Teller Avenue and East 170th Street. The fire extended through all floors of the building. — https://www.nbcnewyork.com/bronx/deli-fire-claremont/6439118/
- The Department of Buildings issued a Full Vacate Order at 1365 Findlay Ave, affecting 32 apartment units and four ground-floor commercial spaces. — https://abc7ny.com/post/fire-crews-battle-5-alarm-bronx-deli/18354424/
- Chief of Department John Esposito said three cars parked on three different fire hydrants delayed the operation. — https://www.nbcnewyork.com/bronx/deli-fire-claremont/6439118/
- A tenant told News 12 that smoke pouring into his home woke him, not a smoke alarm. Mayor Mamdani said a landlord violating the law by not having working smoke detectors in every unit is within the city's control. — https://bronx.news12.com/2-people-hurt-in-massive-fire-at-bronx-deli
- Page angle: this is a ground-floor deli or bodega under apartments. It points to commercial-to-residential fire separation and detection in mixed-use buildings.
- A five-story apartment building off the Grand Concourse caught fire on Feb 2, 2026, leaving at least 82 people homeless. Three frozen hydrants hindered firefighters, and the fire burned through the top floor and cockloft. — https://abc7ny.com/post/firefighters-pull-4-alarm-fire-claremont-bronx-apartment-building/18526493/
- A resident quoted by Hoodline mentioned a neighbor's electrical problem with a light before the fire. This is an anecdote, and no cause has been confirmed. — https://hoodline.com/2026/02/over-80-displaced-as-four-alarm-fire-ravages-bronx-apartment-building-fdny-battles-blaze-amid-frozen-hydrants/
- A fifth-floor apartment fire at 538 Claremont Parkway on Apr 12, 2026 left one resident injured in a mixed-use building. Crews found one person trapped on the fifth floor and carried them out. The report adds that city property records list the building as five stories, mixed-use, with ground-floor storefronts. — https://hoodline.com/2026/04/fifth-floor-bronx-blaze-leaves-resident-hurt-in-claremont-walk-up/
- Flames erupted on the top floor of 1420 Clay Ave and spread between the ceiling and the roof. The building has a full vacate order after a November 2025 fire that injured four people, and fire marshals later determined the cause was electrical wiring. Tenants cite a lack of communication from the landlord, HHH Realty Co. — https://bronx.news12.com/tenants-demand-answers-months-after-fire-at-claremont-building and https://bronx.news12.com/4-injured-in-claremont-3-alarm-fire-including-2-firefighters
- A four-alarm fire ripped through the roof of a six-story building at 1520 Sheridan Ave. The snippet gives no date. — https://bronx.news12.com/body-found-after-massive-four-alarm-fire-tears-through-claremont-apartment-building
- Earlier: a four-alarm top-floor fire at 1686 Clay Ave on Dec 17, 2017 displaced 23 families. — https://bronx.news12.com/crews-battle-4-alarm-fire-on-clay-avenue-37084675
- Pattern across these fires: top-floor and cockloft fires in 5- and 6-story prewar buildings, plus a mixed-use ground-floor start. This is my own observation from the sources, not a source claim.

**NYCHA (Claremont Village Houses)**
- City Limits reported that fire safety is a life-threatening problem at Butler Houses, one of the Claremont Village complexes housing about 11,000 residents. Malfunctioning fire alarms and CO detectors there have long been a hazard. The snippet is undated and the article appears to be several years old. — https://citylimits.org/some-nycha-residents-raise-fire-safety-concerns/
- The same article says NYC law requires NYCHA apartments to have a hard-wired smoke detector with battery backup and a self-closing hallway door, and that all buildings have an alarm system that shows a fire's location to first responders. A resident there wanted detectors in the common hallways on all 21 floors. — same URL
- A fatal fire at 1368 Webster Avenue was started by burning incense, and many residents said they didn't hear smoke alarms. — same URL
- A tenth-floor fire at 1408 Webster Ave, a high-rise NYCHA building, broke out around 3 a.m. It was contained to a single apartment. The snippet gives no date. — https://bronx.news12.com/2-injured-in-claremont-village-building-fire
- NYCHA design guidelines call for two dedicated phone lines to a central station or Fire Command Center, and elevator recall. — https://designguidelines.nycha.info/fire-alarm/

**City data (HPD / 311 / DOB), via aggregator**
- 1365 Findlay Ave (the January 2026 fire building) is a 32-unit, 5-floor building from 1924 with 139 recorded HPD violations, above the Claremont Village average of 93.3. It also had heat and hot water complaints from January 2025. — https://augrented.com/nyc/2027830042-1365-findlay-avenue
- 1326 Grand Concourse (56 units, 5 stories) has 734 recorded HPD violations, with mold, water damage and pest complaints in 2026. — https://augrented.com/nyc/2028310013-1326-grand-concourse
- 200 Marcy Place (6 stories, 55 units, built 1924) has 569 recorded HPD violations. — https://augrented.com/nyc/2028310020-200-marcy-place
- 1410 Morris Ave (63 units, 6 floors, 1941) has 334 recorded HPD violations. It also has 587 311 calls and 42 DOB complaints, against neighborhood averages of 88.3 and 4.9. — https://augrented.com/nyc/2027860002-1410-morris-avenue
- 415 Claremont Parkway is managed by Housing Works Inc and has 96 open violations. — https://www.openigloo.com/building/nyc/2009418-2028970132/bronx-claremont-415-claremont-parkway-10457
- Neighborhood benchmark: the aggregator's Claremont Village average is 93.3 HPD violations, 88.3 311 calls, 4.9 DOB complaints and 2.7 permits (7 years) per building. — https://augrented.com/nyc/2027830042-1365-findlay-avenue
- DOB permits (fire alarm / FDNY filings) for Claremont: EMPTY. Only the aggregated permit counts above were found.
- 311 fire-alarm-specific complaints for Claremont: EMPTY.

**Code and rules**
- Owners must provide and install at least one approved operational smoke and CO detector in each apartment, and tenants maintain them. — https://hpdsigns.nyc/smoke-and-carbon-monoxide-detectors-nyc/
- FDNY says smoke alarms go outside each sleeping room, within 15 feet of the door, with one inside each sleeping room in newer buildings. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/fdny/downloads/pdf/business/Support/smoke-carbon-monoxide-alarms-english.pdf
- Where the building code requires detectors in dwelling units, they must be annunciated by unit at a constantly attended location. — https://codelibrary.amlegal.com/codes/newyorkcity/latest/NYCadmin/0-0-0-171825
- A contractor blog (lower-quality source) says existing units can comply with approved sealed-battery detectors, while hard-wired, interconnected installation applies to new construction and substantial alterations. Verify this against the code before using it. — https://zentrades.pro/zenfire/blog/new-york-smoke-alarm-law-smoke-detectors-carbon-monoxide-rules-and-owner-duties
- LPC rules affecting Claremont installs: EMPTY. I found no landmark district in Claremont.

**Entry, wiring and AV conditions**
- A local security vendor's page claims many Claremont buildings last had a full intercom replacement in the 1990s, and some still run on original copper wiring threaded through plaster walls. It also says the NYCHA developments use NYCHA-standard M&S, Lee Dan or TekTone hardware. These are vendor claims, not independently verified. — https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/door-buzzer-repair-claremont-bronx-ny
- The same vendor's fire alarm page describes pre-war five- and six-story walk-ups with open stairwells that act as vertical chimneys. That is generic Bronx copy. Its claim that the 2022 Twin Parks fire "exposed gaps in Claremont apartment buildings" looks wrong. Its own text puts that fire on East 181st Street, which is not Claremont. Do not reuse the claim. — https://abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-claremont-bronx-ny.html
- Not verified in sources: Claremont-specific entry, wiring or AV conditions beyond those vendor claims.

**Businesses and blocks where it matters**
- Findlay Avenue between Teller Avenue and East 170th Street: the Jan 2026 deli fire. — https://www.nbcnewyork.com/bronx/deli-fire-claremont/6439118/
- Claremont Parkway: 538 Claremont Parkway has ground-floor storefronts under five floors of apartments. The shop owner Hamed Saadeh was left sweeping up shattered glass from his storefront. — https://hoodline.com/2026/04/fifth-floor-bronx-blaze-leaves-resident-hurt-in-claremont-walk-up/
- Clay Avenue (1420 and 1686 Clay Ave), Sheridan Avenue (1520), Webster Avenue (1368 and 1408 NYCHA) and Grand Concourse (1326): see the fire and violation entries above.
- Webster Avenue has a proliferation of storefront mosques and travel agents catering to West African immigrants. That is a mix of storefront-level commercial uses. — https://theneighborhoods.substack.com/p/claremont-the-bronx

**Local news and Reddit**
- Local news: see the News 12, ABC7, NBC, Gothamist and Hoodline items above. The Gothamist piece reports a full vacate order and DEP asbestos testing on site. — https://gothamist.com/news/2-injured-in-5-alarm-fire-at-bronx-apartment-building-fdny-says
- Reddit threads about fire alarms in Claremont: EMPTY.

### 2. Neighborhood background

**History and development**
- Claremont was once part of the 2,000-acre Morris estate. Gouverneur Morris II auctioned off much of his land in 1848, aware of suburban development with the arrival of the railroad. — https://theneighborhoods.substack.com/p/claremont-the-bronx and https://www.nycgovparks.org/parks/claremont-park/history
- The mansion's builders are disputed. The Parks Department credits the Zborowski de Montsaulain couple, who built a mansion in 1859. A neighborhood history says Martin Zabriskie built it in 1858. — https://6tocelebrate.org/site/claremont-park/ and https://theneighborhoods.substack.com/p/claremont-the-bronx
- The 1884 New Parks Act led to the city's 1888–90 purchase of land for Claremont and other parks. The mansion was razed in 1938 and replaced with a gazebo. — https://www.nycgovparks.org/parks/claremont-park/highlights/11710
- Transit: the Claremont Parkway station on the Third Avenue El opened Sept 19, 1888 and closed Apr 29, 1973. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claremont_Parkway_station. The New York Central's Claremont Park station closed June 1, 1960. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claremont_Park_station_(New_York_Central_Railroad)
- Claremont Parkway was Bathgate Place in 1850, Wendover Place in 1890, and took its current name in 1913. — https://forgotten-ny.com/2016/10/crotona-park-high-bridge-bronx-part-2/
- The Claremont Village Houses were completed in 1959 under NYCHA, spanning more than 18 acres with high-rise red-brick towers and low-rise row houses. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/claremont-village. A different account says four developments housing over 10,000 residents were built in the early 1960s. — https://theneighborhoods.substack.com/p/claremont-the-bronx
- Several synagogues in the surrounding blocks were repurposed as Christian churches. — same substack URL

**Main streets and corridors**
- Webster, Clay and Fulton Avenues are the eastern corridors, and the Grand Concourse is the western boundary. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/claremont
- Claremont Parkway runs west across Claremont Village to the park at Webster Avenue. — https://forgotten-ny.com/2016/10/crotona-park-high-bridge-bronx-part-2/
- The Seven Light Baptist Church at Third and Claremont looks like a repurposed bank building. — same URL

**Building stock**
-

## Concourse, Bronx — /fire-alarm-installation-concourse-bronx-ny (factory research, 2026-10-27)

**A. Fire alarm installation in Concourse: local angles**

- Recent fires on the Concourse spine, with building type and scale. At 1215 Grand Concourse (the 167th St corner), a five-story mixed-use building had a top-floor fire just after midnight on Feb. 2, 2026. It became a four-alarm fire with more than 230 fire and EMS personnel responding. DOB issued a full vacate order after finding charred roof joists and parts of the roof open to the elements. The fire burned through the top floor and cockloft. ABC7 places this building in Claremont, while CBS describes it as Grand Concourse and 167th, so label it "Grand Concourse at 167th" on the page. — https://www.cbsnews.com/newyork/news/bronx-fire-two-hurt-fdny/ and https://abc7ny.com/post/firefighters-pull-4-alarm-fire-claremont-bronx-apartment-building/18526493/
- 1166 Grand Concourse (off McClellan St), a six-story building. Flames broke out in a third-floor apartment just after 3 p.m. Ten people were injured, two critically. Those two were found unconscious from smoke inhalation on an upper floor, not in the fire apartment. Date is not shown in the snippet; the page was indexed about 355 days before 2026-10-27. This supports a smoke-spread-to-upper-floors angle. — https://abc7ny.com/post/bronx-fire-10-people-injured-breaks-6-story-building/18113463/
- Carbon monoxide event inside Concourse. On March 18, 2026, several people were overcome by CO on Grant Avenue between E. 164th and E. 165th Streets. The FDNY chief believes a broken boiler caused it. Seven people were injured, two seriously, in a three-story building. Firefighters' own CO detectors went off as they entered. This is relevant for a CO-detection upsell angle. — https://abc7ny.com/post/7-people-injured-2-carbon-monoxide-concourse-bronx-apartment-building/18730393/
- Concourse Village fire, Mar 23, 2026. At 1011 Sheridan Ave, firefighters battled flames on the sixth floor for just over 30 minutes. — https://bronx.news12.com/2026/03/24/person-hospitalized-following-concourse-village-apartment-fire/54YBD1b0sBzxlUMBq8VUGL
- Vacant-building fire, May 30, 2026. The call came in at 1:41 a.m. for a fire on the first and second floors of a three-story vacant building at 843 Walton Ave. It drew 46 units and 141 FDNY and EMS personnel. — https://bronx.news12.com/fire-rips-through-first-and-second-floors-of-concourse-village-building
- High-rise on Concourse Village East. An older CBS report (undated in the snippet) says a fire on the 15th floor of a 25-story building at Concourse Village East and East 156th Street sent flames out of the balcony. Confirms high-rise stock exists in the Concourse Village area. — https://www.cbsnews.com/amp/newyork/news/bronx-high-rise-fire
- NYCHA fire in Concourse, undated older report. A dozen families were displaced by a fire at a NYCHA building on Findlay Avenue in the Concourse section. Date not shown, so do not cite as recent. — https://cbsnews.com/newyork/news/bronx-nycha-apartment-fire
- Older Grand Concourse fire: a top-floor fire at 2700 Grand Concourse, a six-story building, killed two men. The Fire Department said the apartment had no working smoke alarm. 2700 Grand Concourse is in the Kingsbridge Heights/Bedford Park area, outside Concourse proper. Use only as a borough-level Grand Concourse example, and the date is unknown. — https://www.cbsnews.com/newyork/news/2-killed-in-bronx-apartment-fire/
- Large Concourse-corridor fires spread through the cockloft. In a five-alarm fire at the Grand Concourse near E. 172nd St (which is Tremont/Claremont, not Concourse; date unknown), at least two of the building's nine wings caught fire, and it spread to the cockloft. — https://cbsnews.com/amp/newyork/news/5-alarm-fire-bronx-apartment
- Building-stock and wiring angle for the pre-war stock. By the mid-1930s almost 300 apartment buildings stood along the Concourse. They were customarily five or six stories high, with wide entrance courtyards bordered by grass and shrubs. The wide-courtyard layout is a site-access and wiring-routing detail for this building type. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Concourse_(Bronx)
- Wiring and retrofit claims are installer marketing, not neutral research. One Bronx installer says the corridor has 6- to 12-story buildings with ornate lobbies, shared mechanical spaces and aging electrical systems, and that retrofitting through plaster-over-concrete walls without damaging historic finishes requires wireless mesh technology and careful conduit routing. The same vendor says Notifier's SWIFT wireless platform is FDNY-approved. Treat as vendor claims and verify before reusing. — https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-bronxwood-bronx-ny
- When NYC residential buildings need a fire alarm system: for Group R-2 apartments, when any dwelling unit is three or more stories above the lowest exit to a street, when any unit is more than one story below the highest exit, or when the building has more than 16 dwelling units. The five-to-six-story Concourse apartment houses fall in this range. This is a secondary engineering-firm source, so verify against the NYC Building Code (BC 907). — https://www.builtengineers.com/post/when-are-fire-alarm-systems-required-in-residential-buildings
- NYC process layers. The framework has three layers: BC 907 (DOB, system design and document submission), FC 907 (FDNY, inspection frequency, technician certification and monitoring stations), and NFPA 72. Also: BC 907 requires fire alarm documents to be submitted to and approved by the Fire Department before the system is installed. — https://www.tfp1.com/blog/fdny-fire-alarm-inspection-requirements/ and https://www.wmfireprotection.com/code-compliance.php
- Testing and inspection. FDNY requires annual testing of fire alarm systems per NFPA 72, performed by FDNY-approved companies. One source claims a semi-annual functional testing requirement for most systems, which conflicts with the annual statement. Verify before publishing either. — https://www.komplyos.com/guides/fdny-compliance and https://www.tfp1.com/blog/fdny-fire-alarm-inspection-requirements/
- 2017 Bronx fire policy changes still bear on installs. Int 0602-2018 requires doors in R-1 and R-2 residential buildings to be self-closing by July 31, 2021. Int 1256-2018 requires fire-safety plans for residential occupancies with three or more dwellings in mixed-use buildings. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Bronx_apartment_fire
- DOB/FDNY permit data (limited). FDNY's plan-application status list (submitted after 06/01/2019, dated 9/9/2019) includes fire alarm plan applications at 260 E 161st St, Bronx. That address is in the Concourse Plaza/161st St retail node. The list is dated and shows no outcome, so it is only evidence that fire alarm filings occur on 161st St. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/fdny/downloads/pdf/business/plan-application-status.pdf
- Local-data angle: HPD violation data on Concourse buildings (third-party aggregators of city data, not HPD itself; verify on HPD Online before citing).
  - 760 Grand Concourse: 227 recorded HPD violations, above the Concourse Village average of 102.8. DOB and HPD conducted multiple inspections in 2025 on safety complaints, including egress issues. — https://augrented.com/nyc/2024580072-760-grand-concourse-bronx
  - 910 Grand Concourse: 211 recorded HPD violations against the Concourse Village average of 102.8. — https://augrented.com/nyc/2024610001-910-grand-concourse-bronx
  - 1235 Grand Concourse (ZIP 10452): 29 open HPD violations, 9 of them Class C (immediately hazardous), as of Aug 2026. It is a 130-unit building built in 1929. — https://weverit.com/building/ny/new-york/1235-grand-concourse-bronx-10452
  - Violations from this sort of data include defective fire egress, such as bars or gates on windows to the fire escape. That example is 2790 Grand Concourse, which is outside Concourse (ZIP 10458). — https://weverit.com/building/ny/new-york/2790-grand-concourse-bronx-10458
- LPC context for work in the district. An LPC review is under way for upgrades at 1130 Grand Concourse (Carolyn McLaughlin Community Center, in the Grand Concourse Historic District, at McClellan St). Scope includes bronze-finished signage, recessed digital display monitors and updated exterior lighting. This shows exterior-visible work in the district goes through LPC. — https://newyorkyimby.com/2026/01/lpc-to-review-proposal-for-1130-grand-concourse-in-concourse-village-the-bronx.html
- Local fire-alarm business/vendor pages exist, but none is specific to Concourse's blocks. One Bronx installer lists the Grand Concourse corridor from 138th Street to Mosholu Parkway as a service area. — https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-bronx-ny
- Reddit threads about fire alarms/entry systems in Concourse: EMPTY (none found).
- 311 complaint data specific to fire alarms in Concourse: EMPTY.
- DOB permit data for Concourse fire alarm installs: EMPTY (only the dated 2019 FDNY list above).
- LPC rules on interior or in-wall fire alarm work in the district: EMPTY (not found in sources).

**B. Neighborhood background**

- History: Bronx Borough Engineer Louis Risse proposed the boulevard in the 1890s. The Grand Concourse was begun in 1894 and completed in 1909. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/concourse
- Cost and design: built 1894–1909 during the City Beautiful Movement at a cost of $14 million. Risse designed it as a four-mile thoroughfare divided into three roadways by tree-lined dividers. It opened to traffic on November 25, 1909. — https://www.brickunderground.com/blog/2015/12/grand_concourse_bronx and https://hdc.org/hdc-across-nyc/bronx/hd-map/bronx-landmarks-grand-concourse/
- Southern extension: it initially ran from 161st Street to Mosholu Parkway, and was extended south to 138th Street in 1927 when Mott Avenue was widened and renamed. — https://www.welcome2thebronx.com/2020/05/the-grand-concourse-a-look-at-the-bronxs-most-famous-street/
- Subway-driven building boom: the IND Concourse Line opened in 1933, and by the mid-1930s almost 300 apartment buildings had been built along the Concourse. Developers built Renaissance Revival and Art Deco apartment buildings for a middle-class population, predominantly Jewish, Italian and Irish families. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Concourse_(Bronx) and https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/concourse
- Style timeline from LPC via Brick Underground: Tudor, Renaissance and Colonial Revival designs between 1922 and 1931, then Art Deco and Modern between 1935 and 1945. The emphasis on light and air in larger apartments was prompted by the Tenement House law of 1901. — https://www.brickunderground.com/blog/2015/12/grand_concourse_bronx
- Decline and non-conforming storefronts: during the 1970s–80s decline, non-conforming ground-floor storefronts appeared on many buildings; before then, stores weren't permitted on the Concourse. In the 1960s the grassy medians were replaced with green-painted cement. — https://www.welcome2thebronx.com/2020/05/the-grand-concourse-a-look-at-the-bronxs-most-famous-street/ and https://kids.kiddle.co/Grand_Concourse_(Bronx)
- Neighborhood definition: a neighborhood in the southwestern Bronx including the Bronx County Courthouse, the Bronx Museum of the Arts and Yankee Stadium. Population was 75,371 in 2010, with ZIP codes 10451 and 10452. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concourse,_Bronx
- Main streets and commercial corridors: large retail nodes on 161st Street and adjacent blocks at Concourse Plaza, and smaller nodes on 165th and 167th Streets. The Grand Concourse itself forms the eastern boundary per one neighborhood guide. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concourse,_Bronx and https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/concourse-neighborhood/
- Retail and anchors: a Key Food by Yankee Stadium, and the Bronx Terminal Market mall at the south end with a Target and big-box stores. — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/concourse-neighborhood/
- Building stock along the Concourse by segment (Brick Underground):
  - Northern section: diverse architecture including single-family homes.
  - Middle section: 167th to 149th Street, the "civic heart of the Bronx", with the Bronx Supreme Court and Borough President's office.
  - Cluster of Art Deco co-ops: around 167th Street.
  - Lower section: 149th St to 138th St, mostly residential with more co-op buildings.
  — https://www.brickunderground.com/blog/2015/12/grand_concourse_bronx
- Individual building examples. 675 Walton Ave is a 12-story condominium built in 1936 with 83 units. 800 Grand Concourse is a 7-story brick building completed in 1955 with 254 units. Executive Towers is currently the tallest building on the Grand Concourse. — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/concourse-village-neighborhood/ and https://www.welcome2thebronx.com/2020/05/the-grand-concourse-a-look-at-the-bronxs-most-famous-street/
- Grand Concourse Historic District (NYC landmark district, designated October 25, 2011; LP-2403). It contains 61 apartment houses built between 1917 and 1959, two parks, and public institutions including the Bronx County Courthouse and Andrew Freedman Home (both individual landmarks). The proposal covered the Concourse between East 167th and East 153rd Streets and properties along Walton Avenue west of Franz Sigel and Joyce Kilmer Parks. It was also added to the National Register on August 24, 1987. — https://s-media.nyc.gov/agencies/lpc/lp/2403.pdf, https://www.citylandnyc.org/grand-concourse-historic-district-proposed-2/ and https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Concourse_(Bronx)
- Other nearby landmark district: the Morris Avenue Historic District is two blocks west of the Grand Concourse at East Tremont Avenue. That is outside Concourse proper, in the Tremont/University Heights area. — https://a860-gpp.nyc.gov/downloads/5m60qs40c?locale=en
- Parks:
  - Joyce Kilmer Park: home to the Heinrich Heine (Lorelei) Fountain, a white marble sculpture from the late 1800s.
  - Franz Sigel Park: 16 acres, one of the neighborhood's most popular recreation spots.
  - Macombs Dam Park: a community park with multiple sports courts and athletic fields, including the Joseph Yancey Track and Field.
  - Lower Concourse Park: slated to open in 2026 along the Harlem River (verify opening status).
  — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/concourse-village-neighborhood/ and https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/concourse-neighborhood/
- Landmarks and institutions: Yankee Stadium (opened in

## East Tremont, Bronx — /fire-alarm-installation-east-tremont-bronx-ny (factory research, 2026-10-27)

**Caveats**
- No Reddit threads were found, so that field is EMPTY.
- No source tied LPC rules, or any home-technology or AV detail, to East Tremont specifically.

### A. Fire alarm installation: neighborhood-specific facts

**Building types and what the work meets**
- Mixed-use buildings with a store on the ground floor and apartments above are an East Tremont Avenue pattern. A 7-alarm fire started in a furniture store on the ground floor of a four-story building at East Tremont and Commonwealth Avenues, and 23 people were hurt. Date not shown in the snippet. — https://cbsnews.com/amp/newyork/news/bronx-fire-furniture-store-seven-alarm
- In that same fire, a resident said a bolted window guard stopped her from using the fire escape. A resident also said she woke to smoke alarms. This supports pitching alarm audibility and coverage in upper-floor units, and egress checks. — https://cbsnews.com/amp/newyork/news/bronx-fire-furniture-store-seven-alarm
- Housing is described as largely rental, and most of it as subsidized or income-restricted. Co-op conversion mostly didn't happen in the Bronx. — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/east-tremont-neighborhood/
- A neighborhood-guide source describes East Tremont's housing as prewar apartment buildings, rowhouses and mid-century infill. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/east-tremont
- A junk-removal vendor page (low-authority) describes dense 5–6 story prewar walk-ups on Webster, 3rd, Park and Belmont Avenues. It also describes 6-story prewar elevator co-ops on Crotona Park East. I would treat these as unverified. — https://junkrabbit.nyc/junk-removal-tremont
- The Twin Parks development (2,250 apartments, three schools, three day care centers) spans the East Tremont, Tremont and Fordham neighborhoods. Twin Parks East sits next to Bronx Park. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twin_Parks
- The 2022 fire was in Twin Parks North West at 333 E 181st St, a 19-story, 120-apartment building. The sources place it in Tremont or Fordham Heights, not East Tremont proper. It is still the main local reference point for high-rise fire alarm work. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Bronx_apartment_fire
- Retrofit and wiring conditions: EMPTY. No source described conduit, plaster, or masonry conditions in East Tremont buildings.

**Alarm lessons from the 2022 fire**
- Smoke alarms were present throughout 333 E 181st St, and the first 911 call came from a neighbor who heard an alarm on the floor. — https://gothamist.com/news/5-alarm-fire-the-bronx-tremont
- Residents said the alarms often went off falsely. Some ignored the warning because of it. This is a strong angle for false-alarm reduction, panel maintenance and clear signaling. — https://www.newsweek.com/bronx-building-residents-say-fire-alarm-often-went-off-ignored-sound-before-deadly-blaze-1667613
- Fire officials said every death was from smoke inhalation, and the fire stayed in the apartment of origin and the adjacent hallway. A door that did not self-close let smoke spread through the building. — https://www.norwoodnews.org/a-look-back-on-legislative-actions-taken-on-the-2nd-anniversary-of-the-twin-parks-fire/
- Local Law 111 requires doors to interior corridors or stairs to be self-closing. Failing to maintain them is a Class C ("immediately hazardous") violation. — https://council.nyc.gov/press/council-votes-on-legislative-package-to-strengthen-fire-safety-inspired-by-tragic-twin-parks-fire-in-bronx
- The 2022 laws shortened the correction period to 14 days, set penalties of $250–500 plus $250 a day after the deadline, and restricted landlord self-certification. — https://council.nyc.gov/press/council-votes-on-legislative-package-to-strengthen-fire-safety-inspired-by-tragic-twin-parks-fire-in-bronx
- A Gothamist review a year after the fire found city enforcement of self-closing door violations lagging. — https://gothamist.com/news/one-year-after-deadly-twin-parks-fire-in-the-bronx-tenants-move-away-and-enforcement-lags

**City data: HPD, 311 and DOB (East Tremont addresses)**
- OpenIgloo lists roughly 1,000 apartment buildings under "East Tremont" with open-violation counts. The count shown differs between pages (994 vs. 1,004). It includes some West Farms addresses, so don't cite a firm number. — https://www.openigloo.com/buildings/east-tremont/violations?page=6
- Examples from that list, each with 14–15 open violations: 2058 Washington Ave, 1992 Belmont Ave, 894 Fairmount Pl, 1980 Bathgate Ave, 2106 Hughes Ave, 1917 Crotona Ave and 2297 Southern Blvd. These are third-party aggregations of city data. — https://www.openigloo.com/buildings/east-tremont/violations?page=6
- 1230 East Tremont Ave (10460) is a 60-unit building from 1932 with 87 open HPD violations, 47 of them Class C. One open Class C violation concerns defective self-closing doors, and it also has 14 open DOB violations. Data as of Sep–Oct 2026. — https://weverit.com/building/ny/new-york/1230-east-tremont-avenue-bronx-10460
- 868 East Tremont Ave (10460) is a 1911 walk-up with 13 open HPD violations, 6 of them Class C. One open violation is garbage in a public stairwell. — https://weverit.com/building/ny/new-york/868-east-tremont-avenue-bronx-10460
- 2220 East Tremont Ave (ZIP 10462, east of the neighborhood core) is a 12-story building with 13 DOB permits on record, 12 of them active. It has 86 violations, 30 open, including HPD violations for a panic bar on a roof door that blocks re-entry and a defective stair latch. This shows the kind of egress hardware that sits alongside alarm work. — https://regwatch.nyc/address/2220-east-tremont-ave-bronx-ny
- A citywide HPD point: self-closing door violations numbered 37,917 in 2022, and 15,040 were still open about a year after the Twin Parks fire. — https://gothamist.com/news/one-year-after-deadly-twin-parks-fire-in-the-bronx-tenants-move-away-and-enforcement-lags
- 311 complaint data for East Tremont as a whole: EMPTY. Only per-building figures appeared. For example, a source cites 38 HPD complaints at 1230 E Tremont against a neighborhood average of about 15. — https://weverit.com/building/ny/new-york/1230-east-tremont-avenue-bronx-10460

**Permit and code process (FDNY and DOB)**
- FDNY fire alarm applications require a NY State registered design professional (PE or RA) to approve the plans. — https://nyc-business.nyc.gov/nycbusiness/description/fire-alarm-application/apply
- Documents needed include a Certificate of Occupancy or approved Schedule A, floor plans, a riser diagram and an operating sequence matrix. Landmark compliance documents are required if applicable. — https://nyc-business.nyc.gov/nycbusiness/description/fire-alarm-application/apply
- The design must follow NYC Building Code 2014, NYC Electrical Code 2011 and NFPA 72 (2010 edition). — https://nyc-business.nyc.gov/nycbusiness/description/fire-alarm-application/apply
- Under the NYC Admin Code, older fire alarm systems installed under earlier rules are accepted as long as they are maintained in good working order. Some exceptions apply. — https://nycadmincode.readthedocs.io/t27/c01/sch17/art05/
- NYC Fire Code 907.3: an approved alarm system must follow the Building Code, the Electrical Code and NFPA 72 as modified by FC Appendix B. — https://up.codes/viewer/new_york_city/nyc-fire-code-2022/chapter/9/fire-protection-systems
- Under the 2022 NYC Building Code, where a fire alarm system is required, carbon monoxide detectors with sounder bases go in any room containing CO-producing equipment. — https://up.codes/viewer/new_york_city/nyc-building-code-2022/chapter/9/fire-protection-systems
- FDNY's Fire Code guide says an Auxiliary Radio Communication (ARC) system requirement for existing buildings is typically triggered by alterations. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/fdny/downloads/pdf/business/nyc-fire-code-guide.pdf
- LPC rules for East Tremont: EMPTY. The only landmarked district found is Morris Avenue Historic District, which Wikipedia places in Tremont along Morris Ave between East Tremont Ave and E 179th St. It consists of attached brownstones. It is likely west of the East Tremont boundary, so confirm before using it. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tremont,_Bronx

**Local news (fires on or near East Tremont Avenue)**
- A 3-alarm fire at Randall and East Tremont Avenues spread through two 2-story commercial buildings and hit Fabulous Flowers and Ikonic Sports Bar. This is in Throgs Neck, far east of the neighborhood. — https://bronx.news12.com/firefighters-battle-3-alarm-fire-in-commercial-area-of-throgs-neck
- Nov 8, 2025: a 5-alarm fire at an auto shop on East Tremont Ave, between E 180th St and Commonwealth Ave, was reported by Fox 5. It was in the Van Nest/Parkchester area, and part of the back of the building collapsed. — https://www.fox5ny.com/news/4-firefighters-injured-battling-5-alarm-fire-bronx-auto-shop
- That fire spread to two nearby homes and displaced more than a dozen residents. — https://ny1.com/nyc/all-boroughs/public-safety/2025/11/10/bronx-auto-body-shop-fire-displaces-more-than-a-dozen-residents
- The address differs by outlet: 1740 East Tremont (News 12) versus 1470 East Tremont (amNY). It appears to be the same event, so verify before citing an address. — https://www.amny.com/police-fire/five-alarm-fire-bronx-building-11082025/
- A 5-alarm fire on the sixth floor of a 100-unit building at 1920 Walton Ave in Tremont. The date is not in the snippet. — https://www.cbsnews.com/newyork/news/bronx-walton-avenue-fire/
- A Tremont brownstone fire on Prospect Avenue killed three. A WCBS report mentioned complaints about wiring and illegal subdivisions. Date not shown. — https://www.cbsnews.com/newyork/news/three-killed-in-bronx-house-fire
- Another Tremont fire on the Grand Concourse started on the first floor of a five-story building. No injuries, date not shown. — https://www.cbsnews.com/newyork/news/fire-tremont-apartment-building

**Reddit**
- EMPTY. No relevant Reddit thread surfaced.

**Competitor reference**
- A competitor page (Abstract Enterprises, 460 E Fordham Rd) uses generic Bronx copy with a mixed-use bodega scenario along East Tremont. It is not neighborhood research, and I would not rely on it. — https://abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-east-tremont-bronx-ny.html

### B. Neighborhood background

**History and development**
- The land was once owned by the Lorillard family of tobacco fame. From the mid-19th century the area served as a step up from Lower East Side slums for immigrants. German farmers arrived in 1848. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Tremont,_Bronx
- The name "Tremont" appeared in the 1840s. Before urbanization the land was in the Town of West Farms, annexed by NYC in 1874. By the 1880s the street grid was mapped and speculative builders put up frame houses and tenements. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/east-tremont
- Bronx Park opened in 1888 and the Bronx Zoo in 1899. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/east-tremont
- A postmaster coined the name "Tremont" in the mid-19th century. The Bronx municipal building opened in 1897 at East Tremont and Park Avenues. — https://www.lehman.edu/vpadvance/artgallery/arch/neighborhoods/tremont.html
- Growth followed German, Irish, Italian and then Jewish immigration. — https://evaluatingurbanplaces.com/2025/03/07/east-tremont-a-south-bronx-neighborhood-negatively-impacted-by-the-cross-bronx-expressway-yet-still-retains-the-vibrant-tremont-commerical-district/
- The former 3rd Avenue elevated line ran through the neighborhood, and East Tremont Avenue was an early commercial corridor. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/planning/download/pdf/plans-studies/sustainable-communities/bmn/bronx_metro_report/tremont.pdf
- The Cross Bronx Expressway, whose central section was completed in 1960, cut through East Tremont. Robert Caro devotes two chapters of *The Power Broker* to the neighborhood. — https://theneighborhoods.substack.com/p/east-tremont-the-bronx
- Dates for the expressway's construction vary by source: the 1950s (Lehman), 1960 (Substack) and 1945 (a tree-service page). Use "mid-century" or "1950s–60s".
- The expressway displaced thousands of residents. Arson and disinvestment followed in the 1970s, though the area kept much of its historic stock. — https://evaluatingurbanplaces.com/2025/03/07/east-tremont-a-south-bronx-neighborhood-negatively-impacted-by-the-cross-bronx-expressway-yet-still-retains-the-vibrant-tremont-commerical-district/

**Boundaries and administration**
- Wikipedia gives these boundaries: E 180th St, Southern Blvd, the Cross Bronx Expressway and Third Ave. It places the neighborhood in Community Board 6, ZIP codes 10457 and 10460, and the NYPD 48th Precinct. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Tremont,_Bronx
- The NYC SBS commercial district assessment says the main corridors are East Tremont Avenue and East 180th Street. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/sbs/downloads/pdf/neighborhoods/avenyc-cdna-easttremont.pdf
- A 2010 population of 43,423 and a median income of $24,443 are listed on Wikipedia. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Tremont,_Bronx

**Main streets and commercial corridors**
- East Tremont Avenue is a 7-mile street running most of the width of the Bronx. It is split into East and West at Jerome Ave. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tremont_Avenue
- Restaurants on East Tremont Avenue serve Venezuelan, African and Dominican food. Arthur Avenue's Little Italy is to the north. — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/east-tremont-neighborhood/
- Other streets named in sources: Crotona, Arthur and Bathgate Avenues. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/east-tremont
- The Tremont Metro-North station is on East Tremont Ave between Park Avenue North and South. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/planning/download/pdf/plans-studies/sustainable-communities/bmn/bronx_metro_report/tremont.pdf

**Building stock**
- Prewar pieces: a 1931 four-family brick townhouse at 2023 Hughes Ave, and the 1932 and 1911 buildings noted above. — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/east-tremont-neighborhood/
- Newer infill: Phipps Houses' ten-story affordable project at 1176 East Tremont Ave. The article is old (about 14 years). — https://www.citylandnyc.org/175/

**Parks and landmarks**
- Walter Gladwin Park is a 15-acre park just south of East Tremont Ave. Crotona Park is nearby and has a 3.3-acre lake. — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/east-tremont-neighborhood/
- The Bronx River Art Center is on East Tremont Ave. — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/east-tremont-neighborhood/
- The Bronx Zoo and the New York Botanical Garden are nearby cultural institutions. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/sbs/downloads/pdf/neighborhoods/avenyc-cdna-easttremont.pdf
- A tree-service page (low-authority) says Tremont Park was renamed Walter Gladwin Park in 2020. — https://bronxtreeservices.com/east-tremont-tree-services/
- Part of Bronx Park South lies immediately southwest of the Bronx Zoo. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Tremont,_Bronx

**What the neighborhood is known for**
- Its role as a refuge and stepping stone for immigrant families, especially Jewish families, and the damage the Cross Bronx Expressway did to it. — https://theneighborhoods.substack.com/p/east-tremont-the-bronx
- East Tremont Avenue's commercial strip. — https://evaluatingurbanplaces.com/2025/03/07/east-tremont-a-south-bronx-neighborhood-negatively-impacted-by-the-cross-bronx-expressway-yet-still-retains-the-vibrant-tremont-commerical-district/

**Security, home technology and entry systems**
- Neighborhood-specific news on entry systems, intercoms or smart-home adoption: EMPTY.
- A tenant-side report checklist does suggest chec

## Edenwald, Bronx — /fire-alarm-installation-edenwald-bronx-ny (factory research, 2026-10-27)

Several of the angles you asked for returned nothing. Those bullets say EMPTY. I used up my searches and did not get a direct hit on HPD, 311, DOB-permit or Reddit data for Edenwald.

### A. Fire alarm installation in Edenwald

**Building types and entry/wiring situations**
- **Edenwald Houses is the biggest fire alarm "building type" here.** It was established in 1953 and has forty buildings, 3 and 14 stories tall, with 2,039 apartments. NYCHA owns and manages it, and it is the largest development in the Bronx. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edenwald_Houses
- **Unit and building counts differ by source.** Figures run from 2,034 to 2,039 apartments, and Grokipedia says 42 buildings. NYCHA and the Mayor's Office use 2,035 apartments and 40 buildings. — https://www.nyc.gov/site/nycha/about/press/pr-2023/pr-20230628.page
- **A mixed stock of 3-story walk-ups and 14-story elevator buildings** means two different retrofit conditions on one campus. This is a Grokipedia claim, so treat it as secondary. — https://grokipedia.com/page/Edenwald_Houses
- **The PACT rehab is live right now.** Buildings are getting façade repairs, mechanical system modernization, new entryways, enhanced security and access control, full lobby and common area upgrades, and new roofs. — https://nychajournal.nyc/783-million-pact-modernization-project-at-edenwald-houses-moves-forward/
- **Doors and intercoms are being replaced.** The project FAQ says doors will be replaced with new ones that close automatically, and there will be a new intercom system to let guests in. The FAQ also says enhanced security will include site-wide cameras and access control. — https://edenwaldhousesbx.com/faqs/
- **Timeline.** The FAQ expected construction to begin in summer 2023 and finish in summer 2026. Another account says financing closed and construction began in 2023. — https://edenwaldhousesbx.com/faqs/ and https://www.constructionowners.com/news/nycha-completes-8-1m-edenwald-community-center-renovations
- **Latest news (October 2026).** The Mayor's Office reopened the community, senior and child care centers. It describes the wider modernization as including mechanical system upgrades, enhanced security and free development-wide WiFi. — https://www.nyc.gov/mayors-office/news/2026/10/after-years-of-closure--mayor-mamdani-re-opens-edenwald-s-commun
- **Community center cost and property manager.** About $8.1M of the $783M went into the three community centers. — https://citylimits.org/at-edenwald-houses-newly-reopened-senior-center-honors-longtime-resident/ The site's property manager, C+C Management, employs security staff who monitor cameras. — https://nychajournal.nyc/783-million-pact-modernization-project-at-edenwald-houses-moves-forward/
- **Fire alarm scope within the PACT work is EMPTY.** None of the sources I found say whether the project replaces fire alarm panels, detectors or horns/strobes. Do not claim it on the page.
- **Rowhouses, duplexes and cottages outside the NYCHA campus.** The neighborhood has prewar brick rowhouses, modest duplexes, multifamily brick colonials, colonial revival cottages and newly renovated Cape Cod-style townhouses. — https://www.homes.com/bronx-ny/edenwald-neighborhood/houses-for-sale/
- **A sample listing shows the age of this stock.** 1906 Edenwald Ave is a single-family home built in 1920 with two stories. — https://www.homes.com/bronx-ny/edenwald-neighborhood/houses-for-sale/
- **Redfin shows many multi-family units for sale.** The listings include 16 multi-family units, plus 1 condo and 2 townhouses, in one month. That suggests small multi-family and two-family houses are common. Redfin mislabels Edenwald as being in Queens County, so use it only for this rough signal. — https://www.redfin.com/neighborhood/207257/NY/New-York/Edenwald/pending-listings
- **Small low-rise co-ops sit next door in Wakefield and Williamsbridge.** Examples are 825 East 230th Street (Wakefield), a low-rise walk-up and 753 East 227th Street (Williamsbridge). — https://www.nybits.com/apartments/bronx_coop_buildings.html
- **More Wakefield co-ops nearby.** Listings include 745 E 231st St and 740 E 232nd St. One includes an indoor garage. — https://www.homes.com/bronx-ny/wakefield-neighborhood/co-ops-for-sale/
- **Mixed-use buildings with apartments over shops** are only supported by generic Bronx copy, not Edenwald-specific data. Gun Hill Commons (1750–1780 Gun Hill Road, 10469) is a strip shopping center, not mixed-use. — https://locations.uedge.com/gun-hill-commons/

**Local fire news**
- **Fire at a 3-story private residence, 740 E. 227th St.** It was an all-hands fire that started on the second floor. FDNY had trouble with frozen hydrants, and four families were displaced. One tenant thought the cause was electrical, but the official cause was still under investigation in the article. The article has no date in the result, so verify the date before citing. — https://cbsnews.com/amp/newyork/news/fire-rips-through-home-in-edenwald-the-bronx
- **Other Edenwald fire news is EMPTY.** I found no other Edenwald-specific fire stories.

**Regulation that changes how the work is done**
- **FDNY, not DOB, approves fire alarm plans.** A Project Authorization is required after approval of a fire alarm plan, before construction and before scheduling inspection. The fire alarm plan filing requires a licensed Master Electrician and an S97 holder. — https://nyc-business.nyc.gov/nycbusiness/description/fire-alarm-application/apply
- **Inspection and sign-off.** The system must be 100% complete and pre-tested before FDNY inspection. The as-built riser diagram needs a signed Functionality Statement. — https://nyc-business.nyc.gov/nycbusiness/description/fire-alarm-application
- **DOB filing and pro-cert.** Local Law 195 of 2018 removed the need to file fire alarm design plans with DOB. Fire alarm installers and electricians with an FDNY Certificate of Fitness may perform professional certification, but building core fire alarm systems must still be certified by FDNY. — https://metropolisny.com/2023/04/new-fire-alarm-rules-take-effect-on-may-1/
- **Panel replacement and radio systems.** DOB Buildings Bulletin 2024-001 took effect February 9, 2024. A panel can be replaced if the alteration plans are filed with and approved by FDNY. Full replacement in a building that would otherwise need an Auxiliary Radio Communication System (ARCS) still requires one. — https://sparcfp.com/nyc-dob-buildings-bulletin-for-fire-alarm-work-in-existing-buildings/
- **Smoke and CO alarms are needed even where no central system is.** Smoke and carbon monoxide alarms are required in almost all residential occupancies, even when a central fire alarm system isn't. — https://www.builtengineers.com/post/when-are-fire-alarm-systems-required-in-residential-buildings
- **Competitor page for this exact keyword.** Abstract Enterprises Security Systems has an Edenwald page, but its Bronx office is at 460 E Fordham Rd, and most of the copy is generic Bronx text (Grand Concourse, Hunts Point, Twin Parks). It is not Edenwald-specific, so avoid copying its claims. — https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-edenwald-bronx-ny

**City data and community discussion**
- **HPD violations for Edenwald addresses: EMPTY.**
- **311 complaints: EMPTY.**
- **DOB permits: EMPTY.**
- **LPC rules:** EMPTY. I found no LPC historic district in Edenwald. Edenwald Houses was listed on the National Register of Historic Places in 2024, which is a federal listing, not LPC. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edenwald_Houses
- **Reddit threads: EMPTY.** No Reddit results came back for Edenwald.
- **NYCHA-wide context.** A Housing Authority-sourced stat on the unofficial site edenwaldhouses.us says the federal monitor settlement cut emergency work orders. I would not use it, because that site is unofficial and the figure is unverified. — https://edenwaldhouses.us/about-us/

### B. Neighborhood background

**History**
- **Early land.** The Dutch West India Company "purchased" much of the East Bronx from the Siwanoy in 1640. Thomas Pell bought a good portion of the same land in 1654. Edenwald was long considered part of Eastchester. — https://theneighborhoods.substack.com/p/edenwald-the-bronx
- **The Seton estate.** William Seton built "Cragdon," a fifty-one-acre estate in the center of what would become Edenwald. — https://theneighborhoods.substack.com/p/edenwald-the-bronx
- **The name.** John H. Eden owned an estate called Edenwald from 1900 to 1913; in German it means "Eden's Forest." — https://www.nycgovparks.org/parks/edenwald-playground/history
- **Hebrew Orphan Asylum.** After Eden sold the estate, it became a Hebrew Orphan Asylum for children with disabilities. The City acquired the land by condemnation in 1950 to build Edenwald Houses. — https://www.nycgovparks.org/parks/edenwald-playground/history
- **Who built the Houses.** Rodgers & Butler designed it, and Paul Tishman Company started building in 1951 at a cost of roughly $12 million. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edenwald_Houses
- **Rail.** The Gun Hill Road station on the Dyre Avenue Line opened in 1912 for the New York, Westchester and Boston Railway. It closed in 1937 and reopened as part of the subway in 1941. This is the line for the 5 train. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_Hill_Road_station_(IRT_Dyre_Avenue_Line)
- **Low-reliability history source.** CityNeighborhoods.NYC says the name appeared in ads around 1905 and that the Houses had 50 buildings and were completed in 1950. That conflicts with the sources above, so I would not use it. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/edenwald

**Streets and commercial corridors**
- **Major roads.** They include East 233rd, Baychester Avenue, Bronxwood Avenue, Edenwald Avenue, Laconia Avenue, and brief openings on Eastchester and Boston Roads. This is an old page, so verify it. — https://urbanareas.net/info/resources/neighborhoods-bronx/edenwald-bronx-history/
- **Edenwald Avenue.** Redfin lists 95 addresses in 10466 with an average lot size of about 2,756 sq ft. That fits a small-lot house street. — https://www.redfin.com/sitemap/NY/10466/street/Edenwald+Ave
- **Gun Hill Road.** It runs 3.5 miles from Mosholu Parkway to the Hutchinson River Parkway in Baychester. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_Hill_Road_(road)
- **Gun Hill Commons (just south, ZIP 10469).** Tenants include Aldi, Dollar Tree, Dunkin', Petco, Planet Fitness, Rainbow and Ridgewood Savings Bank. — https://locations.uedge.com/gun-hill-commons/
- **Groceries near Edenwald, per Yelp.** They include Pioneer Supermarket and Key Food. — https://www.yelp.com/search?find_desc=Grocery+Store&find_loc=Edenwald%2C+Bronx%2C+NY
- **Named businesses on Edenwald's own corridors: EMPTY.** I found no verified Edenwald-address businesses beyond the grocery names above.

**Parks and landmarks**
- **Seton Falls Park.** It is a 30-acre woodland preserve traversed by what remains of Rattlesnake Creek. — https://theneighborhoods.substack.com/p/edenwald-the-bronx
- **Edenwald Playground** sits next to the Houses. — https://www.nycgovparks.org/parks/edenwald-playground/history
- **47th Precinct and Cardinal Spellman High School** are in the neighborhood. — https://urbanareas.net/info/resources/neighborhoods-bronx/edenwald-bronx-history/

**Known for**
- **Public housing.** The 1950s Houses and the PACT rehab are the headline. See the bullets in section A.
- **Gang history.** In April 2016, authorities raided the Edenwald and Eastchester Houses, indicting 120 people connected to two rival gangs. — https://edenwaldhouses.us/about-us/. Wikipedia says 87 were arrested and 120 charged. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edenwald_Houses. Handle this carefully on a service page.
- **Housing mix beyond the campus.** Homes.com calls it a blue-collar enclave with a central park. — https://www.homes.com/local-guide/bronx-ny/edenwald-neighborhood/
- **Renter-heavy stock.** Most residential real estate is renter-occupied, and many residences were built between 1940 and 1969. — https://www.neighborhoodscout.com/ny/bronx/edenwald

**Nearby context**
- **Baychester Houses** had an earlier PACT renovation across the street from Edenwald. — https://www.nychdc.com/newsroom/city-announces-closing-783-million-pact-modernization-project-edenwald-houses
- **Co-op City** has 15,372 residential units in 35 high-rise buildings and seven townhouse clusters. It borders the area but is a separate neighborhood. — https://www.coopcity.com/

**Not found**
- **Local Reddit discussion on entry systems or home technology: EMPTY.**
- **Non-NYCHA news on building security or entry systems: EMPTY.**

## Edgewater Park, Bronx — /fire-alarm-installation-edgewater-park-bronx-ny (factory research, 2026-10-27)

I ran 8 searches. Items marked EMPTY had no usable result.

### A. Fire alarm installation angles specific to Edgewater Park

- The co-op covers 55 acres and 675 single-family homes with about 3,000 residents. Wikipedia gives 60 acres, so use "roughly 55–60 acres". The market is single-family bungalows and two-story homes, not multi-unit buildings with common-area panels. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/planning/download/pdf/plans-studies/resilient-neighborhoods/edgewater-park/edgewater_park_report_102015.pdf
- The community began as a summer bungalow colony and was adapted for year-round living. Structures sit right next to one another on narrow streets. The report says this tightness inhibits standard resiliency measures. It is a fair inference that it also limits wiring routes and device spacing, but the source doesn't say so. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/planning/download/pdf/plans-studies/resilient-neighborhoods/edgewater-park/edgewater_park_report_102015.pdf
- Summer cottages and bungalows went up in the 1920s and were winterized for year-round use during the Great Depression. Expect old conversions with unknown wiring and added-on rooms. That is my inference, not the source's. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/planning/download/pdf/plans-studies/resilient-neighborhoods/edgewater-park/edgewater_park_report_102015.pdf
- The co-op is served by the City's sanitation and water systems, but it maintains a private sewage network and a volunteer fire company. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/planning/download/pdf/plans-studies/resilient-neighborhoods/edgewater-park/edgewater_park_report_102015.pdf
- The Edgewater Park Volunteer Firehouse is the only volunteer firehouse still operating in the Bronx. Its specialized fleet fits the narrow streets and alleyways where FDNY trucks can't. This is the strongest local reason for working smoke and CO alarms and early monitored notification. — https://www.thecityreporter.nyc/2022/06/13/bronx-edgewater-park-volunteer-firehouse-sandy/
- The volunteer company runs small apparatus. One unit is described as designed for the extremely narrow, winding streets of the waterfront response area. Engine 4, a brush rapid attack truck, was delivered in March 2025. — https://www.unyquefiretrucks.com/New-York-/VFDNY/Edgewater-Park and https://vfanyc.org/edgewater-park-volunteer-hose-company-no-1/
- The firehouse is itself an alarm-work site. A fundraising and project page says funding covers a new fire alarm system, electrical/HVAC upgrades, hurricane-proof windows and two generators. NYS HCR announced a $2.5M resiliency construction project so the firehouse and community center, also a polling place, can serve as a storm shelter. — https://www.unyquefiretrucks.com/New-York-/VFDNY/Edgewater-Park and https://hcr.ny.gov/news/new-york-state-homes-and-community-renewal-announces-25-million-construction-project-bronx
- Flood exposure affects where devices and panels can go. DCP said about 111 Edgewater Park homes are in the flood zone. Sandy left some waterfront homes with up to five feet of flooding. Low-mounted equipment and basement wiring are the risk. — https://www.bxtimes.com/edgewater-parks-resilient-neighborhood-report/ and https://www.thecityreporter.nyc/2022/06/13/bronx-edgewater-park-volunteer-firehouse-sandy/
- A zoning text amendment adopted in October 2013 changed zoning rules so new and existing buildings can add flood protection. The co-op voted to measure building height from the design flood elevation while keeping its 25-foot limit. Elevated homes change how far runs go and where panels and devices can be mounted. That is my inference. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/planning/download/pdf/plans-studies/resilient-neighborhoods/edgewater-park/edgewater_park_report_102015.pdf and https://www.bxtimes.com/edgewater-parks-resilient-neighborhood-report/
- Entry situation: there is only one road in. Listings mention an entrance security booth, cameras and foot patrols. Schedule technicians through the gate and tell residents which vehicles to expect. Check with the co-op for its own rules on contractors. — https://www.apartments.com/local-guide/throgs-neck-edgewater-park-bronx-ny/ and https://www.thisoldhudsonrealestate.com/homes/31-Edgewater-Park/Bronx/NY/10465/177126105/
- Past fire in the community: a two-story home fire on Edgewater Park rose to two alarms on a Monday morning. YouTube titles date it to 3/27/2023 and give the address as 88D Edgewater Park. News12 said the cause was unclear. — https://bronx.news12.com/fire-officials-two-alarm-fire-rips-through-edgewater-park-home and https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rZW6k9TZn1o
- NYC detector law, general and not Edgewater-specific:
  - Every dwelling unit, including one- and two-family homes, needs CO alarms if fossil-fuel equipment is in or next to the building.
  - HPD requires CO detectors in non-owner-occupied one- and two-family homes with fossil fuel devices.
  - CO alarms must be within fifteen feet of the primary entrance to each sleeping room.
  - This matters for basement-entry rentals in the co-op. — https://fdnysmart.org/wp-content/uploads/2015/04/08-smoke-and-cm-4-page-brochure_for-web.pdf and https://www.nyc.gov/site/hpd/services-and-information/detectors.page
- Sales rule: NY law requires functioning CO and smoke detectors in every one- or two-family dwelling, co-op or condo constructed or offered for sale. This is a sales-trigger angle, because Edgewater Park has steady co-op listings (20 co-ops for sale at one point). — https://support.homecoin.com/hc/en-us/articles/9047998445459-New-York-Carbon-Monoxide-and-Smoke-Detector-Installation-Requirement and https://www.homes.com/bronx-ny/throgs-neck-edgewater-park-neighborhood/co-ops-for-sale/
- Competitor note: Abstract Enterprises has an Edgewater Park fire-alarm page. It is generic, it talks about pre-war apartments, NYCHA and the Grand Concourse, none of which fit Edgewater Park. Don't copy its claims. — https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-edgewater-park-bronx-ny
- HPD violations for Edgewater Park — EMPTY. The area is private co-op single-family housing, and I found no data.
- 311 complaints for Edgewater Park — EMPTY.
- DOB permits and fire alarm filings for Edgewater Park — EMPTY. I did not query NYC Open Data.
- LPC rules or a landmark district in Edgewater Park — EMPTY. No landmark designation turned up.
- Reddit threads about Edgewater Park fire or alarms — EMPTY. Search returned no Reddit results. Nextdoor has an Edgewater Park page, but the visible alerts were about unrelated streets such as E 167th St. — https://nextdoor.com/neighborhood/edgewaterparkny--bronx--ny/
- Businesses and blocks where alarms matter:
  - A community deli is listed in the amenities.
  - The community center is another amenity.
  - The DCP report says the co-op is exclusively residential save for a single commercial use. The sentence is cut off in the results, so the commercial use is unconfirmed.
  - The only named streets in Edgewater sources are Main Street, 5 Avenue and Adee Avenue (map labels).
  - Sources: https://www.homes.com/bronx-ny/throgs-neck-edgewater-park-neighborhood/co-ops-for-sale/ and https://www.nyc.gov/assets/planning/download/pdf/plans-studies/resilient-neighborhoods/edgewater-park/edgewater_park_map.pdf

### B. History and development

- George T. Adee built a mansion on the land in 1856. In 1923 the land was leased to Richard W. Shaw Sr., an Irish immigrant who invited church youth to camp there; it became Edgewater Camp. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgewater_Park_(Bronx)
- Campsites turned into bungalows, with a full-fledged bungalow area around 1918. After World War I the community was divided into sections A, B and C, with D and E added later. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgewater_Park_(Bronx)
- The community became a co-op in 1988. The DCP report agrees (residents who rented the land bought it from the primary leaseholder). A Substack source says 1986, so use 1988. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgewater_Park_(Bronx)
- Dates conflict across sources, so cite carefully:
  - Wikipedia says Shaw leased in 1923.
  - A humor-style page says Adee's family sold to Shaw in 1910.
  - Vendor pages say 1910 was "earliest resident-owned co-op". I didn't verify that claim.
  - Sources: https://crackedeggstudios.com/studio/state-of-the-communion and https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/access-control-installation-edgewater-park-bronx-ny
- Shaw let church groups, Boy Scouts and city workers pitch tents and build tarp-covered cottages. Many became year-round homes in the Great Depression. — https://theneighborhoods.substack.com/i/143196229/edgewater
- The volunteer fire department was established in 1922. The City Reporter says it celebrates its 100th anniversary in 2022. A city-neighborhoods page says "mid-century", which conflicts. Use 1922. — https://www.nycfire.net/forums/threads/fdny-and-nyc-firehouses-and-fire-companies-2nd-section.50639/page-168 and https://www.thecityreporter.nyc/2022/06/13/bronx-edgewater-park-volunteer-firehouse-sandy/

### C. Streets, corridors, geography

- The community is bounded by the Throgs Neck Expressway to the south and west, Weir Creek to the north and Eastchester Bay to the east. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/park-of-edgewater
- Throggs Neck is Bronx Community District 10, ZIP 10465, and is patrolled by the 45th Precinct. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Throggs_Neck
- Most streets are unmapped and without sidewalks. Named streets seen in sources include Main Street, 5 Avenue, Adee Avenue, Alden Park, Magnolia Place and Beech Place. The map and listings show these, and the abstractenterprises page lists others I couldn't verify. Pennyfield Avenue (Throggs Neck) is outside the co-op. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/planning/download/pdf/plans-studies/resilient-neighborhoods/edgewater-park/edgewater_park_report_102015.pdf and https://www.splicetoday.com/writing/take-me-to-throgs
- Commercial corridors outside the gate (Pennyfield Ave, Throggs Neck Blvd, Tremont Ave) — EMPTY for fire alarm specifics.
- Transit: an express bus picks up and drops off in the community, plus local buses. — https://www.thisoldhudsonrealestate.com/homes/31-Edgewater-Park/Bronx/NY/10465/177126105/

### D. Building stock

- One listing describes a "bungalow-style" 2-bedroom co-op. Others describe a 1930 ranch-style home (built 1930) and a unit with a bay window, deck and backyard. — https://www.homes.com/bronx-ny/throgs-neck-edgewater-park-neighborhood/co-ops-for-sale/ and https://www.zillow.com/homedetails/189D-Edgewater-Park-D-Bronx-NY-10465/464378832_zpid/
- Address format: unit letters A–D after a number (for example 64A, 88D), matching sections A–E. Homes with letter suffixes appear to be separate cottages. Treat that as an inference. — https://www.redfin.com/neighborhood/209010/NY/New-York/Edgewater-Park
- The median listing price for single-story homes was $335K. The Redfin listing page for Edgewater Park shows data as of Dec 2025. — https://www.redfin.com/neighborhood/209010/NY/New-York/Edgewater-Park/single-story
- A city-neighborhoods page says the stock includes bungalows, shingled cottages, and later brick rowhouses. It is not a rigorous source. Wikipedia and DCP describe single-family homes, so I wouldn't promise rowhouses. — https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/park-of-edgewater
- Landmark districts, walk-ups and high-rises: EMPTY. None found in Edgewater Park.

### E. Parks, landmarks, and what it's known for

- Amenities include two private beaches, basketball courts, boat mooring, parks and a volunteer fire department. — https://www.homes.com/bronx-ny/throgs-neck-edgewater-park-neighborhood/co-ops-for-sale/
- It is jokingly called the "Bronx Riviera", and life centers on the firehouse. — https://www.thecityreporter.nyc/2022/06/13/bronx-edgewater-park-volunteer-firehouse-sandy/
- The volunteer department is one of nine in NYC. The community holds a Ragamuffin parade and a Labor Day Olympics with a 100-year tradition. The fire department also holds an annual Memorial Day parade. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgewater_Park_(Bronx)
- Nearby landmarks in Throggs Neck: Fort Schuyler, built between 1833 and 1851, and Ferry Point Park (413.8 acres). Pelham Bay Park is across Weir Creek. — https://www.splicetoday.com/writing/take-me-to-throgs, https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferry_Point_Park and https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/park-of-edgewater
- Resident profile: many residents are firefighters, police officers and city workers (vendor claim). A neighborhood page also says many residents served as firefighters, police officers, or city workers. Useful tone for fire-safety copy. — https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/door-buzzer-repair-edgewater-park-bronx-ny and https://cityneighborhoods.nyc/park-of-edgewater

### F. Recent news, security and tech

- The City reported (June 2022) that the volunteer firehouse was getting state funding for upgrades years after they were promised. The funding had been at risk that January. — https://www.thecityreporter.nyc/2022/06/13/bronx-edgewater-park-volunteer-firehouse-sandy/
- 2010 fair-housing lawsuit: the Fair Housing Justice Center sued Edgewater Park and Silver Beach Gardens in February 2010. The settlement abolished the three-owner reference rule (the Wikipedia reference title mentions this). Avoid this topic on the service page. — https://ecbawm.com/news/ecbawm-settles-racial-housing-discrimination-case-against-bronx-cooperative/
- Access systems: gate and booth security with cameras and foot patrols come from listings. Smart-home and intercom details for individual homes — EMPTY.
- 2026 news about Edgewater Park fire or building security — EMPTY. One "guide" article (phdns.latahcountyid.gov) is unreliable. It describes Hudson River views and Westchester Avenue access, which don't fit this location. Don't use it.

### G. What would change how installation is done here

- Mostly one- and two-story single-family homes. Expect combination smoke/CO alarms (interconnected or hardwired) rather than commercial addressable panels. Commercial panels are for the deli, community center and firehouse.
- The volunteer fire company has no FDNY-style apparatus, so notification speed and central-station dispatch routing matter. I could not confirm how Edgewater Park alarm signals reach the volunteer department. EMPTY.
- Flood zone: mount equipment above the design flood elevation where possible, and use sealed conduit and corrosion-resistant hardware in bay-edge homes. A vendor page recommends 316 stainless hardware and sealed conduit for flood-zone work (https://www.abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/door-buzzer-repair-edgewater-park-bronx-ny). Treat that as vendor advice.
- Co-op approval for alterations: DCP says there is an active co-op board that administers bylaws. Whether it must approve alarm work is EMPTY. Confirm with the co-op before promising anything. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/planning/download/pdf/plans-studies/resilient-neighborhoods/edgewater-park/summary-report-edgewater-park.pdf?r=1

## Fieldston, Bronx — /fire-alarm-installation-fieldston-bronx-ny (factory research, 2026-10-27)

**Sections 1–3 are about fire alarm installation in Fieldston. Sections 4–9 are general neighborhood background.**

### 1. Fieldston-specific fire alarm angles

**Building types, entry and wiring**
- The Fieldston Historic District is made up of single-family houses and related outbuildings. It contains 257 houses and related structures on winding tree-lined streets with rock outcroppings. — https://culturenow.org/site/e0395126-3bcf-4b1f-8b60-e92830130a5c
- Most of these houses are probably NYC Group R-3 (one- and two-family homes), though that classification is my inference. For R-3, a central fire alarm system is typically not required, but smoke and carbon monoxide alarms may still be required. This makes a house-by-house smoke/CO/interconnected-alarm job the likely service here, not a full FDNY panel job. — https://www.builtengineers.com/post/when-are-fire-alarm-systems-required-in-residential-buildings
- Where a building does need a fire alarm system, plans go to the FDNY Bureau of Fire Prevention, and a Project Authorization is issued before installation. — https://www.builtengineers.com/post/when-are-fire-alarm-systems-required-in-residential-buildings
- The houses are old and architecturally varied, so retrofit wiring is a real concern. The district master plan calls Fieldston a rare, largely intact planned suburban community. Styles include Colonial Revival, Craftsman, Medieval, Tudor, Mediterranean and formal modernist houses. — https://www.nypap.org/preservation-history/fieldston-historic-district/ and https://www.nyc.gov/assets/lpc/downloads/pdf/calendar/01_20_09.pdf
- Examples of early-era houses:
  - A 1918–19 Dutch Colonial Revival at 331 West 250th Street (Baum).
  - A Tudor Revival at 4401–4403 Waldo Avenue (1913, with additions in 1919 and 1927).
  - A 1918 one-family home at 4680 Fieldston Road.

  The sources give construction years but say nothing about wall construction or wiring. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fieldston,_Bronx and https://www.citylandnyc.org/tag/fieldston-historic-district/
- Streets were originally private. By 1914 there were three and a half miles of privately owned streets, maintained by assessments on Fieldston homeowners. Whether they are still private today is not confirmed in my results. — https://www.flickr.com/photos/emilio_guerra/6803416510
- Wikipedia says the neighborhood is owned by a private association. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riverdale,_Bronx
- Apartment-style stock along the edges differs from the houses:
  - The Fieldston, 6035 Broadway, is a six-story, 103-unit post-war co-op built in 1961, with a doorman and live-in super. — https://www.cityrealty.com/nyc/riverdale/the-fieldston-6035-broadway/61624
  - 5500 Fieldston Road ("Fieldstondale") is a 9-story, 188-unit co-op completed in 1960. Redfin and Homes.com place it in North Riverdale, so it is not strictly inside Fieldston. — https://www.homes.com/building/5500-fieldston-rd-bronx-ny/b-vvydkh7y96h0y/
  - Nybits lists these Fieldston co-ops: Greystone Park (3840 Greystone Ave), 255 Fieldston Terrace and 3235 Cambridge Ave, all post-war mid-rise elevator buildings. — https://www.nybits.com/apartments/bronx_coop_buildings.html
  - These post-war co-ops are the likeliest Fieldston buildings to have common-area fire alarm panels. That is my inference.

**Landmarks (LPC) rules that touch an install**
- LPC permits are required for restoration, alteration, reconstruction, demolition or new construction that affects the exterior of a building in a historic district. Ordinary repairs such as replacing broken glass, matching repaints and recaulking do not need a permit. — https://fieldstonhistoricdistrict.com/fpoa
- LPC says most exterior changes to front and rear facades in historic districts require LPC review. — https://www.nyc.gov/site/lpc/about/permits-and-making-alterations.page
- Practical implication (my inference): exterior horn/strobes, conduit runs, roof or exterior communicator antennas, or any change to a visible facade need an LPC check. Interior-only wiring was not addressed in the sources I found.
- A Fieldston master plan exists. It was proposed in 2009 to let LPC staff approve certain alterations that meet its requirements. LPC's 2023 rule amendments list Fieldston work including additions, outbuildings, window replacement, heating, venting and air conditioning, and work on significant landscape improvements. Fire alarm devices are not named. — https://www.nyc.gov/assets/lpc/downloads/pdf/calendar/01_20_09.pdf and https://nyc.gov/assets/lpc/downloads/pdf/Rules/LPC_Proposed_Miscellaneous_Rule_Amendments_Calendared_4-18-23_FINAL.pdf
- LPC does not regulate mature trees in Fieldston because it is a Special Natural Area, where landscape changes need City Planning review. This may matter for trenching to outbuildings or detached garages (my inference). — https://www.nypap.org/preservation-history/fieldston-historic-district/
- LPC staff may issue permits for new work while a Summons is in effect if the work corrects a hazardous condition. — https://irp-cdn.multiscreensite.com/8ec2d52b/files/uploaded/Rules%20of%20the%20NYC%20Landmarks%20Preservation%20Commission_01.22.2019.pdf

**NYC smoke/CO and HPD rules**
- For non-owner-occupied one- and two-family homes, HPD requires at least one approved, operational detector per dwelling unit, including a CO detector within 15 feet of the entrance to each sleeping room. — https://hpdsigns.nyc/smoke-and-carbon-monoxide-detectors-nyc/
- CO alarms need an end-of-life alarm, and owners must replace detectors at the end of their useful life. — https://hpdsigns.nyc/smoke-detecting-devices-notice-28-rcny-12-01/
- Class A multiple dwellings must replace a detector within 30 days if it fails within a year of installation due to a defect. — https://hpdsigns.nyc/smoke-detecting-devices-notice-28-rcny-12-01/

**Local news**
- News 12 reported a fire at 461 W 238th St in Fieldston that took 12 units and 60 firefighters. It started on the first floor just before 6 a.m. A resident woke minutes before his alarm to smoke in his room and had to wake his roommates, who were all asleep. No injuries were reported. The article is about 15 months old. It mentions no alarm sounding, and 238th Street is at the district's southern edge, so treat the location as "Fieldston per News 12." — https://bronx.news12.com/fdny-house-fire-in-fieldston-takes-crew-of-60-to-extinguish
- News 12 also reported on a vacant, boarded-up Riverdale house at 5501 Palisade Ave. More than 100 firefighters responded. Construction openings let fire move between floors, and there was fire in the basement, first floor, second floor and attic. It is in Riverdale, not Fieldston, and is about 10 months old. — https://bronx.news12.com/fdny-battles-flames-shooting-out-of-riverdale-home

**Competing local pages**
- Abstract Enterprises has a templated "Fire Alarm Installation Fieldston Bronx NY" page. Its content is generic Bronx copy, such as Grand Concourse co-ops and Art Deco buildings, and nothing in it is Fieldston-specific. A page built on landmarked houses and private streets would stand apart. — https://abstractenterprisessecuritysystems.com/fire-alarm-installation-fieldston-bronx-ny.html

**Not found**
- HPD violations, 311 complaints and DOB permits specific to Fieldston — EMPTY
- Reddit threads about Fieldston fire alarms or security — EMPTY. My searches returned no Reddit results.
- Named local fire alarm businesses and specific blocks where alarms matter — EMPTY

### 2. Schools and institutions (fire alarm relevance not documented)
- Fieldston is home to two of the three "Hill Schools": Horace Mann School and Ethical Culture Fieldston School. Manhattan University sits on Manhattan College Parkway, the southern boundary. Institutional fire alarm specifics — EMPTY. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fieldston,_Bronx

### 3. Home technology and entry systems
- Fieldston-specific data on intercoms, smart-home use or AV — EMPTY. The "near-me-services" pages with reviews for Fieldston are templated and I did not use them. — EMPTY

### 4. History and development
- Joseph Delafield bought more than 250 acres in 1829, set up a small lime quarry and built a family vacation house. — https://www.brownstoner.com/upstate/fieldston-bronx-history-architecture/
- Interest grew after the IRT subway reached West 242nd Street in 1908, and Delafield's heirs decided to develop. They first sold nine acres to Teachers College of Columbia University (now Horace Mann School) at West 246th Street. — https://www.flickr.com/photos/emilio_guerra/6803416510
- Civil engineer Albert E. Wheeler laid out streets and utilities, including water, gas, sewers and later electricity. He consulted Frederick Law Olmsted and James R. Croes to build a winding-road layout that used the natural topography. The layout was finalized in 1914. — https://artsandculture.google.com/asset/fieldston-historic-district/HwFgnJJkxEd8YQ?hl=en
